# Food sources and arithmetic

Generated by `node scripts/build.mjs` from data/foods.json and data/ingredients.json. Do not edit by hand: change the data and rebuild.

Energy per ingredient is IFCT 2017's own value (kJ converted at 4.184 kJ per kcal). Pure fats (oils, ghee) have no energy value in our IFCT copy, so they use IFCT's fat factor of 37 kJ per g (about 8.84 kcal per g). Ingredients not in IFCT use USDA FoodData Central or the maker's label, as listed.

## Roti (/calories/roti/)

Recipe: 100 g atta and about 70 g water, rolled into rotis.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, atta | 100 | 320.3 | 10.6 | 64.2 | 1.5 | IFCT 2017 A019 |
| Water | 70 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| **Total** | | **320.3** | **10.6** | **64.2** | **1.5** | |

Cooked yield 133 g (atta x (1 - 0.111) / (1 - 0.33): IFCT's atta is 11.1% water and USDA's cooked chapati is 33% water). Per 100 g cooked: 240.8 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 phulka (about 24 g atta): 32 g cooked = 75 kcal, P 3 g, C 15 g, F 0 g
- 1 medium roti (about 30 g atta): 40 g cooked = 95 kcal, P 3 g, C 19 g, F 0 g
- 1 large roti (about 40 g atta): 53 g cooked = 130 kcal, P 4 g, C 26 g, F 1 g
- 1 medium roti with 1 tsp ghee (5 g): 40 g cooked + 5 g Ghee = 140 kcal, P 3 g, C 19 g, F 5 g
- 2 medium rotis: 80 g cooked = 190 kcal, P 6 g, C 39 g, F 1 g
- 2 rotis with ghee: 80 g cooked + 10 g Ghee = 280 kcal, P 6 g, C 39 g, F 11 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- 2 rotis and a katori of dal tadka: 2*roti:medium+dal-tadka:k200 = 305 kcal, P 10 g, C 49 g, F 7 g
- 3 rotis and a katori of mixed veg: 3*roti:medium+mixed-veg:k200 = 435 kcal, P 14 g, C 71 g, F 10 g

Outside (Dhaba or restaurant, with ghee): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 5 to 10 g Ghee = 140 to 185 kcal. Basis: No analysed data was found for restaurant or tandoori rotis, so this is a stated assumption: the same roti brushed with 1 to 2 NIN teaspoons (5 ml each) of ghee. Butter has slightly less fat than ghee, so butter rotis sit at or below this range.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: phulka: 80 kcal per a phulka, ours 77 (+4%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Judgement calls:

- Medium roti = 30 g atta: NIN's cereal portion is 30 g raw (100 kcal). NIN's phulka at 80 kcal works out to about 24 g of atta. Large = 40 g is a judgement for bigger Punjabi-style rotis.
- The textbook chapati (The Art & Science of Cooking via INDB, ASC096) is 80 g atta and 1 tsp ghee for 4 chapatis: 20 g atta each, 72.8 kcal with its share of ghee. INDB's per-100 g value (202 kcal) is not used as a cross-check because INDB doesn't allow for water lost in cooking, so it is per 100 g of dough, not of cooked roti.
- Cooked yield 1.33 is derived from water contents (IFCT atta 11.1%, USDA chapati FDC 171844 33%). No Tier 1-3 source weighed a home roti directly.
- USDA's commercial chapatis (FDC 171844, 297 kcal per 100 g; FDC 174075, 299 kcal) are not used for values: they are factory-made and contain added fat.
- Teaspoon = 5 ml (NIN); 5 g of ghee taken as one teaspoon, matching NIN's 5 g fat portion of 45 kcal.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central 171844, chapati or roti, plain. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/171844/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Khurshid et al., chapati quality study, Food Science and Biotechnology, 2020. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6992807 (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Plain rice (/calories/plain-rice/)

Recipe: 100 g raw milled rice, cooked until the water is absorbed.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rice, raw, milled | 100 | 356.4 | 7.9 | 78.2 | 0.5 | IFCT 2017 A015 |
| **Total** | | **356.4** | **7.9** | **78.2** | **0.5** | |

Cooked yield 298 g (2.98 times the raw weight, the measured average for boiled long-grain polished rice (Bognár 2002)). Per 100 g cooked: 119.6 kcal. Katori density 0.67 g per ml (USDA FoodData Central lists 1 cup (236.6 ml) of cooked white rice as 158 g).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 101 g cooked = 120 kcal, P 3 g, C 26 g, F 0 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 134 g cooked = 160 kcal, P 4 g, C 35 g, F 0 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 168 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 4 g, C 44 g, F 0 g
- 1 plate (100 g raw rice): 298 g cooked = 355 kcal, P 8 g, C 78 g, F 1 g
- 100 g cooked: 100 g cooked = 120 kcal, P 3 g, C 26 g, F 0 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- Dal chawal (a katori of rice, a katori of dal tadka): plain-rice:k200+dal-tadka:k200 = 275 kcal, P 8 g, C 45 g, F 6 g
- Rajma chawal (a katori of rice, a katori of rajma): plain-rice:k200+rajma:k200 = 370 kcal, P 12 g, C 58 g, F 9 g
- Chole chawal (a katori of rice, a katori of chole): plain-rice:k200+chole:k200 = 425 kcal, P 13 g, C 61 g, F 12 g
- Kadhi chawal (a katori of rice, a katori of kadhi): plain-rice:k200+kadhi:k200 = 260 kcal, P 7 g, C 42 g, F 7 g

Outside (Restaurant, finished with ghee): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 0 to 5 g Ghee = 160 to 205 kcal. Basis: No analysed data was found for restaurant steamed rice. Stated assumption: the same rice with 0 to 1 NIN teaspoon of ghee on top.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: rice, 1 cup (200 ml): 170 kcal per a 200 ml katori, ours 160 (+6%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FoodData Central 168878, white rice, long-grain, cooked: 130 kcal per 100 g, ours 120 (+9%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/168878/nutrients
- Indian Nutrient Databank, Boiled rice (ASC113): 117.2 kcal per 100 g, ours 120 (-2%). 351.6 kcal per plate of 100 g raw parboiled rice. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-
- Bognár 2002, analysed boiled rice: 105 kcal per 100 g, ours 120 (-12%). Calculated from the report's analysed values; wetter, drained rice. https://publikationen.bibliothek.kit.edu/9642002/1928

Judgement calls:

- Yield 2.98 (boiled) chosen over 2.94 (stewed); the difference is about 1%.
- Katori density from USDA's cup weight, because no Indian source gives grams of cooked rice per katori. NIN's 200 ml cup at 170 kcal implies a similar density.
- Plate = 100 g raw rice, the textbook plate (The Art & Science of Cooking via INDB ASC113: 100 g parboiled rice, 200 ml water, 1 plate). IFCT raw milled rice (A015) and parboiled (A014) differ by about 1%.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Bognár, Tables on weight yield of food and retention factors of food constituents, 2002. https://publikationen.bibliothek.kit.edu/9642002/1928 (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central 168878, rice, white, long-grain, cooked. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/168878/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Dal tadka (/calories/dal-tadka/)

Recipe: one textbook bowl, 30 g toor dal in 300 ml water with a 10 g ghee tadka.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Red gram dal (toor, arhar) | 30 | 99.2 | 6.5 | 16.6 | 0.5 | IFCT 2017 B021 |
| Water | 300 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Ghee | 10 | 88.4 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 10.0 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| Ginger, fresh | 5 | 2.8 | 0.1 | 0.4 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G014 |
| Chillies, green | 3.3 | 1.4 | 0.1 | 0.2 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G008 |
| Cumin seeds | 2.1 | 6.4 | 0.3 | 0.5 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 G025 |
| Garlic | 0.6 | 0.7 | 0.0 | 0.1 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G011 |
| **Total** | | **199.0** | **7.0** | **17.8** | **10.9** | |

Cooked yield 351 g (the ingredients added together, with no allowance for water boiled off, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 56.7 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (thin dal taken as 1 g per ml, like water; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 85 kcal, P 3 g, C 8 g, F 5 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 115 kcal, P 4 g, C 10 g, F 6 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 250 g cooked = 140 kcal, P 5 g, C 13 g, F 8 g
- Half a cup (100 ml): 100 g cooked = 55 kcal, P 2 g, C 5 g, F 3 g
- 1 textbook bowl (30 g dry dal): 351 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 7 g, C 18 g, F 11 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- Dal tadka with rice (a katori of each): dal-tadka:k200+plain-rice:k200 = 275 kcal, P 8 g, C 45 g, F 6 g
- Dal tadka with 2 rotis: dal-tadka:k200+2*roti:medium = 305 kcal, P 10 g, C 49 g, F 7 g

Outside (Restaurant or dhaba): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1.5 to 2 = 170 to 225 kcal. Basis: Published restaurant-style recipes use about the same fat per gram of dal as this textbook dal: Swasthi's dal tadka 2 tbsp ghee plus 1.5 to 2 tbsp oil for 200 g dal (0.24 to 0.27 g per g), against 10 g ghee for 30 g dal here (0.33 g per g). The difference per katori comes mostly from a thicker dal, so the range is 1.5 to 2 times the dal per katori. This is a stated assumption.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: plain dal, half a cup: 100 kcal per half a cup, ours 57 (+76%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- Indian Nutrient Databank, Arhar with spinach (BFP172): 52.8 kcal per 100 g, ours 57 (-7%). Same textbook recipe with 50 g spinach, so slightly lower. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-

Range shown on page: NIN's plain dal (100 kcal per 100 ml) is much thicker than the textbook dal (about 57 kcal per 100 ml). Shown as a range on the page.

Judgement calls:

- No textbook recipe for plain dal tadka exists in INDB; the closest is Basic Food Preparation's arhar dal with spinach (BFP172). Spinach removed.
- Cup = 240 ml for the textbook water (INDB's unit table), so 1.25 cups = 300 ml.
- Yield = sum of ingredients (no evaporation), following INDB. Real dal loses some water, so this sits at the thin end; the range covers thicker dal.
- Density 1 g per ml is an assumption for thin dal.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Swasthi, Dal tadka (Swasthi's Recipes). https://www.indianhealthyrecipes.com/dal-tadka/ (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Rajma (/calories/rajma/)

Recipe: one textbook bowl, 30 g dry rajma with tomato, onion and 1.5 tsp oil.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rajmah, red (dry) | 30 | 89.8 | 6.0 | 14.6 | 0.5 | IFCT 2017 B020 |
| Water | 48.6 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Tomato, ripe, hybrid | 50 | 9.4 | 0.4 | 1.6 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 D075 |
| Onion, big | 25 | 12.0 | 0.4 | 2.4 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 G017 |
| Groundnut oil | 6.8 | 60.1 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 6.8 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Ginger, fresh | 3.2 | 1.8 | 0.1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G014 |
| Garlic | 1.3 | 1.6 | 0.1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G011 |
| **Total** | | **174.7** | **6.9** | **19.1** | **7.5** | |

Cooked yield 165 g (cooked beans at USDA's yield for boiled kidney beans (2.62 times the dry weight), plus the other ingredients). Per 100 g cooked: 105.9 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (rajma curry taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 160 kcal, P 6 g, C 17 g, F 7 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 210 kcal, P 8 g, C 23 g, F 9 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 250 g cooked = 265 kcal, P 10 g, C 29 g, F 11 g
- 1 textbook bowl (30 g dry rajma): 165 g cooked = 175 kcal, P 7 g, C 19 g, F 8 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- Rajma with rice (a katori of each): rajma:k200+plain-rice:k200 = 370 kcal, P 12 g, C 58 g, F 9 g
- Rajma with 2 rotis: rajma:k200+2*roti:medium = 405 kcal, P 15 g, C 62 g, F 10 g

Outside (Dhaba or restaurant): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 5 to 5 g Ghee, plus 0 to 15 g Cream, single (about 19% fat) = 255 to 285 kcal. Basis: From published recipe proportions (Swasthi, Rajma masala): a dhaba-style tadka of 1.5 tbsp ghee for 4 servings (about 5 g each), and a creamy restaurant version with a quarter cup of cream for 4 servings (about 15 g each). Proportions only, not a measured value.

Cross-checks:

- Indian Nutrient Databank, Kidney bean curry (ASC165), per bowl: 172.4 kcal per a bowl, ours 175 (-1%). Same textbook recipe, so this confirms the arithmetic rather than the recipe. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-

Judgement calls:

- INDB's per-100 g value (143.7 kcal) is per 100 g of raw ingredients with no water, so it isn't comparable per gram; the per-bowl value is used instead.
- Teaspoon of oil = 4.5 g (USDA). Bean yield 2.62 from USDA FDC 175193 (raw, 333 kcal) and 175194 (boiled, 127 kcal).
- Density 1 g per ml is an assumption. Spices under 1 g left out.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central 175194, kidney beans, red, boiled. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/175194/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Swasthi, Rajma masala (Swasthi's Recipes). https://www.indianhealthyrecipes.com/rajma-recipe-rajma-masala/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- All India Radio, Bhaderwah rajma GI tag, 2023. https://www.newsonair.gov.in/bhaderwah-rajma-ramban-sulai-honey-of-jammu-and-kashmir-get-gi-tag (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Poha (/calories/poha/)

Recipe: 40 g dry poha with 30 g potato, 10 g peanuts and 10 g oil.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rice flakes (poha) | 40 | 141.5 | 3.0 | 30.7 | 0.5 | IFCT 2017 A011 |
| Potato, brown skin, big | 30 | 20.9 | 0.5 | 4.5 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 F006 |
| Groundnut (peanut) | 10 | 52.0 | 2.4 | 1.7 | 4.0 | IFCT 2017 H012 |
| Groundnut oil | 10 | 88.4 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 10.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Mustard seeds | 2 | 10.2 | 0.4 | 0.3 | 0.8 | IFCT 2017 H013 |
| **Total** | | **313.1** | **6.2** | **37.2** | **15.3** | |

No cooked weight used (no published cooked weight for poha, so portions are counted by dry poha). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 small bowl (20 g dry poha): 0.5 of the recipe = 155 kcal, P 3 g, C 19 g, F 8 g
- 1 plate (40 g dry poha): 1 of the recipe = 315 kcal, P 6 g, C 37 g, F 15 g
- 1 large plate (60 g dry poha): 1.5 of the recipe = 470 kcal, P 9 g, C 56 g, F 23 g
- 1 very large plate (100 g dry poha): 2.5 of the recipe = 785 kcal, P 15 g, C 93 g, F 38 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- A plate of poha and a cup of chai: poha:plate+masala-chai:cup2 = 375 kcal, P 8 g, C 48 g, F 17 g

Outside (Stall or restaurant): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.5, plus 0 to 4.5 g Groundnut oil = 315 to 510 kcal. Basis: No analysed data was found for stall or restaurant poha. Stated assumption: 1 to 1.5 times the textbook plate with up to 1 tsp (4.5 g) more oil.

Cross-checks:

- Indian Nutrient Databank, Poha (BFP044), per bowl: 160.8 kcal per a small bowl, ours 157 (+3%). Same textbook recipe, so this confirms the arithmetic rather than the recipe. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-

Judgement calls:

- ICMR-NIN's 270 kcal per 200 ml cup can't be compared directly, because no source gives the dry poha in a cup of cooked poha.
- Recipe-site plates run larger: Archana's Kitchen uses 300 g poha for 3 (100 g each), so the 100 g row is included.
- Lemon (7.5 g) and spices under 1 g are left out; together they add under 5 kcal.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Archana's Kitchen, Maharashtrian kanda poha. https://archanaskitchen.com/maharashtrian-kanda-poha (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Idli (/calories/idli/)

Recipe: 60 g parboiled rice, 20 g urad dal and 120 ml water, for 8 small idlis.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rice, parboiled, milled | 60 | 211.0 | 4.7 | 46.3 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 A014 |
| Black gram dal (urad) | 20 | 64.8 | 4.6 | 10.2 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 B003 |
| Water | 120 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| **Total** | | **275.8** | **9.3** | **56.5** | **0.7** | |

Cooked yield 200 g (the batter weight, with no allowance for steaming loss, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 137.9 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 small idli (10 g rice and dal): 25 g cooked = 35 kcal, P 1 g, C 7 g, F 0 g
- 1 medium idli (15 g rice and dal): 38 g cooked = 50 kcal, P 2 g, C 11 g, F 0 g
- 1 large idli (20 g rice and dal): 50 g cooked = 70 kcal, P 2 g, C 14 g, F 0 g
- 2 medium idlis: 75 g cooked = 105 kcal, P 3 g, C 21 g, F 0 g
- 4 small idlis: 100 g cooked = 140 kcal, P 5 g, C 28 g, F 0 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- 2 idlis with sambar: 2*idli:medium+sambar:k200 = 305 kcal, P 10 g, C 43 g, F 9 g
- 4 idlis with sambar: 4*idli:medium+sambar:k200 = 410 kcal, P 14 g, C 64 g, F 10 g

Outside (Restaurant, with ghee): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.5, plus 0 to 5 g Ghee = 105 to 200 kcal. Basis: No analysed data was found for restaurant idli. Stated assumption: idlis 1 to 1.5 times the medium size, with up to 1 NIN teaspoon of ghee or podi oil.

Cross-checks:

- Indian Nutrient Databank, Idli (ASC144), per idli: 34.5 kcal per a small idli, ours 34 (0%). Same textbook recipe. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-
- USDA FoodData Central, FNDDS Idli (55702000), 38 g idli: 48.6 kcal per an idli, ours 52 (-6%). 128 kcal per 100 g x 38 g. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2708346/nutrients
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: idli, 150 kcal for 2: 75 kcal per a large idli, ours 69 (+9%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Range shown on page: Textbook idli (INDB ASC144) is 10 g of rice and dal, about 35 kcal; NIN counts 2 idlis at 150 kcal (75 each). Both are shown, matched to small and large rows.

Judgement calls:

- Small = textbook idli (10 g dry). Medium = 15 g dry, which matches USDA's 38 g idli. Large = 20 g dry, close to NIN's idli.
- Yield = batter weight (no steaming loss). Lab idlis are about 63 to 68% water (seen in a search snippet only), which is consistent with 200 g from 80 g of rice and dal.
- USDA's idli fibre (5.8 g per 100 g) looks high for a cooked idli and isn't used.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, FNDDS Idli (55702000). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2708346/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Durgadevi and Shetty, sensory profile of idli, Journal of Food Science and Technology, 2014. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4152537 (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Masala dosa (/calories/masala-dosa/)

Recipe: 2 dosas from 60 g parboiled rice and 20 g urad dal, with a 100 g potato filling; tawa oil per dosa added in the table.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rice, parboiled, milled | 60 | 211.0 | 4.7 | 46.3 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 A014 |
| Black gram dal (urad) | 20 | 64.8 | 4.6 | 10.2 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 B003 |
| Water | 120 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Potato, brown skin, big | 100 | 69.8 | 1.5 | 14.9 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 F006 |
| Onion, big | 75 | 36.0 | 1.1 | 7.2 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 G017 |
| Groundnut oil | 4.5 | 39.8 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 4.5 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Mustard seeds | 1.7 | 8.7 | 0.3 | 0.3 | 0.7 | IFCT 2017 H013 |
| **Total** | | **430.0** | **12.3** | **78.8** | **6.3** | |

No cooked weight used (no published cooked weight for masala dosa, so portions are counted per dosa). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 masala dosa, 1 tsp oil on the tawa: 0.5 of the recipe + 4.5 g Groundnut oil = 255 kcal, P 6 g, C 39 g, F 8 g
- 1 masala dosa, 2 tsp oil on the tawa: 0.5 of the recipe + 9 g Groundnut oil = 295 kcal, P 6 g, C 39 g, F 12 g
- 1 masala dosa, 1 tbsp oil on the tawa (textbook): 0.5 of the recipe + 13.6 g Groundnut oil = 335 kcal, P 6 g, C 39 g, F 17 g
- 1 plain dosa, 1 tsp oil, no filling: 0 of the recipe + 30 g Rice, parboiled, milled + 10 g Black gram dal (urad) + 4.5 g Groundnut oil = 180 kcal, P 5 g, C 28 g, F 5 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- A masala dosa with sambar: masala-dosa:tsp+sambar:k200 = 455 kcal, P 13 g, C 61 g, F 17 g

Outside (Restaurant, butter or ghee): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.5, plus 5 to 15 g Ghee = 300 to 515 kcal. Basis: No analysed data was found for a single restaurant masala dosa. Stated assumption: 1 to 1.5 times the size, with 1 to 3 teaspoons (5 to 15 g) of butter or ghee. A BMJ study of restaurant meals in Bangalore measured full meals, not single dosas, so it isn't used here.

Cross-checks:

- Indian Nutrient Databank, Masala dosa (ASC146), per dosa: 345.1 kcal per a dosa, ours 335 (+3%). Same textbook recipe, so this confirms the arithmetic rather than the recipe. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: masala dosa: 200 kcal per a dosa, ours 255 (-22%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Range shown on page: NIN's 200 kcal per masala dosa against about 345 kcal for the textbook dosa with 1 tbsp tawa oil (INDB ASC146 gives 345 for the same recipe). Shown as a range on the page.

Judgement calls:

- The textbook also tempers the filling with 1 tsp each of chana and urad dal; at an assumed 4 g each they would add about 13 kcal per dosa. Left out because no source gives their weight.
- Tawa oil shown as 1 tsp, 2 tsp and 1 tbsp (USDA: 4.5 g and 13.6 g).
- No Tier 1-3 source independent of the textbook gives a value for a home masala dosa; NIN's 200 kcal is the only one, and it is lower.
- Plain dosa row = half the batter with 1 tsp oil.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central 171411, soybean oil. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/171411/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Samosa (/calories/samosa/)

Recipe: 6 samosas from 60 g maida, 150 g potato and 75 g peas, deep-fried.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 60 | 211.1 | 6.2 | 44.6 | 0.5 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Ghee | 9 | 79.6 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 9.0 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| Water | 45 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Potato, brown skin, big | 150 | 104.7 | 2.3 | 22.3 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 F006 |
| Peas, fresh | 75 | 61.0 | 5.4 | 8.9 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 D061 |
| Groundnut oil | 12 | 106.1 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 12.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Groundnut oil | 50 | 442.1 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 50.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **1004.6** | **14.0** | **75.8** | **71.9** | |

No cooked weight used (no published cooked weight for these samosas, so portions are counted per samosa). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 samosa: 0.1667 of the recipe = 165 kcal, P 2 g, C 13 g, F 12 g
- 1 large samosa (1.5 times): 0.25 of the recipe = 250 kcal, P 3 g, C 19 g, F 18 g
- 2 samosas: 0.3333 of the recipe = 335 kcal, P 5 g, C 25 g, F 24 g

Outside (Street or shop): fat sources x 1.6 to 1.65, portion x 0.7 to 1.2 = 160 to 285 kcal. Basis: Calibrated to Gupta et al. 2016, which measured vendor samosas: a Delhi urban slum samosa of 46.66 g with 13.62 g fat, and a village samosa of 79.25 g with 23.46 g fat (about 29 g fat per 100 g). Size factor = vendor weight against this page's samosa; fat factor chosen so total fat matches the measured fat.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: samosa: 200 kcal per a samosa, ours 167 (+19%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Range shown on page: NIN's 200 kcal per samosa is about 19% above this page's home samosa; street samosas measured by Gupta et al. hold more fat. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Frying oil taken up: solved so that total fat is 18% of the raw samosa weight plus absorbed oil (58.5 g raw per samosa, 3.7 g fat before frying, about 8.4 g absorbed). Moisture lost in frying is not counted. Based on Sakhale et al. 2011 (18.34% oil, fresh oil, lab) and USDA FNDDS samosa (17.47 g fat per 100 g).
- INDB's samosa values (577 kcal per 100 g) count all 480 ml of frying oil as eaten and are not used.
- Teaspoon of ghee = 4.5 g; 3 tbsp water = 45 g. Spices under 1 g left out.
- The Health Ministry's 2025 oil and sugar boards say a samosa can hold 17 to 28 g of fat (news report); consistent with the street range.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Gupta et al., Unhealthy fat in street and snack foods, Journal of Nutrition Education and Behavior, 2016. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4826272 (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Sakhale et al., oil uptake in samosa, Journal of Food Science and Technology, 2011. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3551041 (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, FNDDS Samosa (58124500). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2708730/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Paneer butter masala (/calories/paneer-butter-masala/)

Recipe: 2 bowls from 100 g paneer, 100 g tomato, 50 g onion, 3 tsp butter and 2 tsp cream.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Paneer | 100 | 257.9 | 18.9 | 12.4 | 14.8 | IFCT 2017 L003 |
| Tomato, ripe, hybrid | 100 | 18.9 | 0.8 | 3.2 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 D075 |
| Onion, big | 50 | 24.0 | 0.8 | 4.8 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 G017 |
| Ginger, fresh | 20 | 11.0 | 0.4 | 1.8 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 G014 |
| Garlic | 1.3 | 1.6 | 0.1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G011 |
| Chillies, green | 3.3 | 1.4 | 0.1 | 0.2 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G008 |
| Butter | 14.2 | 101.8 | 0.1 | 0.0 | 11.5 | USDA FDC 173430 |
| Cream, single (about 19% fat) | 10 | 19.5 | 0.3 | 0.4 | 1.9 | USDA FDC 170857 |
| Sugar, white | 6.3 | 24.4 | 0.0 | 6.3 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| **Total** | | **460.5** | **21.4** | **29.3** | **28.8** | |

Cooked yield 305 g (the ingredients added together, with no allowance for water cooked off, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 151.0 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 225 kcal, P 11 g, C 14 g, F 14 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 300 kcal, P 14 g, C 19 g, F 19 g
- 1 textbook bowl (50 g paneer): 153 g cooked = 230 kcal, P 11 g, C 15 g, F 14 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 150 kcal, P 7 g, C 10 g, F 9 g

Outside (Restaurant serving): fat sources x 1.5 to 2, portion x 1.5 to 2.5 = 390 to 725 kcal. Basis: Size: Sanjeev Kapoor's recipe serves 125 g of paneer per person, 2.5 times the textbook bowl's 50 g. Fat: a stated assumption that restaurants finish with 1.5 to 2 times the butter and cream. No lab measurement of restaurant paneer butter masala was found.

Cross-checks:

- Indian Nutrient Databank, Paneer in butter sauce (ASC222), per bowl: 228.3 kcal per a bowl, ours 230 (-1%). Same textbook recipe, so this confirms the arithmetic rather than the recipe. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-

Judgement calls:

- Paneer from IFCT 2017 (L003, 258 kcal per 100 g). Amul's label paneer is 312 kcal per 100 g; branded paneer can be richer.
- Butter teaspoon = 4.73 g (USDA tablespoon 14.2 g / 3). Cream teaspoon = 5 g (USDA tablespoon 15 g / 3). Sugar teaspoon = 4.2 g (USDA).
- Textbook 'single cream' matched to USDA light cream (about 19% fat).
- Monash University's paneer butter masala (469 kcal per serve) is a different, richer recipe and isn't used as a cross-check.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central 173430 (butter), 170857 (light cream), 169655 (sugar). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/173430/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Sanjeev Kapoor, Paneer butter masala. https://www.sanjeevkapoor.com/recipe/Paneer-Butter-Masala.html (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Masala chai (/calories/masala-chai/)

Recipe: one cup, 50 ml toned milk and about 150 ml water, with tea and spices.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Milk, toned (3% fat) | 50 | 29.0 | 1.5 | 2.4 | 1.5 | Amul Taaza label |
| Water | 150 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| **Total** | | **29.0** | **1.5** | **2.4** | **1.5** | |

No cooked weight used (counted per cup; tea leaves and spices add under 1 kcal). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 cup, no sugar: 1 of the recipe = 30 kcal, P 2 g, C 2 g, F 2 g
- 1 cup, 1 tsp sugar: 1 of the recipe + 4.2 g Sugar, white = 45 kcal, P 2 g, C 7 g, F 2 g
- 1 cup, 2 tsp sugar: 1 of the recipe + 8.4 g Sugar, white = 60 kcal, P 2 g, C 11 g, F 2 g
- 1 cutting chai (half a cup), 1 tsp sugar: 0.5 of the recipe + 4.2 g Sugar, white = 30 kcal, P 1 g, C 5 g, F 1 g
- 1 cup with 50 ml buffalo milk, 2 tsp sugar: 0 of the recipe + 50 g Milk, whole, buffalo + 8.4 g Sugar, white = 85 kcal, P 2 g, C 13 g, F 3 g
- 2 cups of chai, 2 tsp sugar each: 2 of the recipe + 16.8 g Sugar, white = 125 kcal, P 3 g, C 22 g, F 3 g
- 3 cups of chai, 2 tsp sugar each: 3 of the recipe + 25.2 g Sugar, white = 185 kcal, P 5 g, C 32 g, F 5 g

Outside (Tapri or station chai): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 0 to 4.2 g Sugar, white, plus 0 to 25 g Milk, whole, buffalo = 60 to 105 kcal. Basis: No measured data was found for tapri or station chai. Stated assumption: the same cup with up to 1 more level teaspoon of sugar and up to 25 ml more buffalo milk.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: tea, 2 tsp sugar and 50 ml toned milk: 75 kcal per a cup, ours 62 (+22%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Range shown on page: NIN's tea (2 tsp sugar, 50 ml toned milk) is 75 kcal; with USDA's 4.2 g level teaspoon it works out to about 62 kcal (-17%). Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Level teaspoon of sugar = 4.2 g (USDA FDC 169655). NIN likely counts about 5 g per teaspoon, which closes most of the gap; Slate shows both ends.
- Toned milk from the Amul Taaza label (58 kcal, 3 g fat per 100 ml), taken as per 100 g. FSSAI toned milk: at least 3.0% fat, 8.5% SNF.
- INDB's hot tea (ASC001) uses 25 ml whole milk and 1 tsp sugar per cup (34 kcal); a lighter cup than NIN's.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Amul Taaza toned milk, label via Dudhsagar Dairy. https://dudhsagardairy.coop/dairy/products/amul-taaza/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Phulka (/calories/phulka/)

Recipe: 80 g atta kneaded with about 60 ml water.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, atta | 80 | 256.2 | 8.5 | 51.3 | 1.2 | IFCT 2017 A019 |
| Water | 60 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| **Total** | | **256.2** | **8.5** | **51.3** | **1.2** | |

Cooked yield 106 g (atta x (1 - 0.111) / (1 - 0.33): IFCT's atta is 11.1% water and USDA's cooked chapati is 33% water, the same method as the roti page). Per 100 g cooked: 241.7 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 small phulka (20 g atta): 27 g cooked = 65 kcal, P 2 g, C 13 g, F 0 g
- 1 phulka (about 24 g atta): 32 g cooked = 75 kcal, P 3 g, C 15 g, F 0 g
- 1 phulka with ½ tsp ghee (2.5 g): 32 g cooked + 2.5 g Ghee = 100 kcal, P 3 g, C 15 g, F 3 g
- 1 phulka with 1 tsp ghee (5 g): 32 g cooked + 5 g Ghee = 120 kcal, P 3 g, C 15 g, F 5 g
- 2 phulkas: 64 g cooked = 155 kcal, P 5 g, C 31 g, F 1 g
- 3 phulkas: 95 g cooked = 230 kcal, P 8 g, C 46 g, F 1 g
- 4 phulkas: 127 g cooked = 305 kcal, P 10 g, C 62 g, F 1 g

Outside (Thali restaurant or tiffin service): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 2.5 to 5 g Ghee = 100 to 120 kcal. Basis: Stated assumption: no measured data was found for restaurant or tiffin phulkas. Same size as a home phulka, brushed with half to one NIN teaspoon of ghee.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: phulka: 80 kcal per a phulka, ours 77 (+4%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Judgement calls:

- Phulka = 24 g atta, the same size as the phulka on the roti page; NIN's 80 kcal phulka works out to about 25 g of atta.
- Small phulka = the textbook chapati (ASC096: 80 g atta for 4, so 20 g each).
- Ghee per phulka (half and one teaspoon) is a typical range, not from the textbook, which uses 1 tsp ghee across 4 chapatis.
- Cooked yield uses the same water-content method as the roti page (IFCT atta 11.1% water, USDA chapati FDC 171844 33% water).

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Plain paratha (/calories/plain-paratha/)

Recipe: 80 g atta and 2 tablespoons of oil, making 3 parathas.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, atta | 80 | 256.2 | 8.5 | 51.3 | 1.2 | IFCT 2017 A019 |
| Groundnut oil | 27.2 | 240.5 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 27.2 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Water | 60 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| **Total** | | **496.8** | **8.5** | **51.3** | **28.4** | |

Cooked yield 148 g (the atta's solids plus the oil, at the 33.5% water of USDA's cooked paratha: (80 x 0.889 + 27.2) / 0.665). Per 100 g cooked: 335.7 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 plain paratha (27 g atta, 2 tsp oil): 49 g cooked = 165 kcal, P 3 g, C 17 g, F 9 g
- 2 plain parathas: 99 g cooked = 330 kcal, P 6 g, C 34 g, F 19 g
- 1 paratha with 1 tsp butter on top (5 g): 49 g cooked + 5 g Butter = 200 kcal, P 3 g, C 17 g, F 14 g
- 1 paratha with 1 tsp ghee on top (5 g): 49 g cooked + 5 g Ghee = 210 kcal, P 3 g, C 17 g, F 14 g
- 100 g cooked paratha: 100 g cooked = 335 kcal, P 6 g, C 35 g, F 19 g
- 2 parathas with curd: 99 g cooked + 100 g Curd (dahi), whole milk = 390 kcal, P 9 g, C 39 g, F 22 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- 2 parathas and a cup of chai: plain-paratha:two+masala-chai:cup2 = 395 kcal, P 7 g, C 45 g, F 20 g

Outside (Dhaba or restaurant): fat sources x 1 to 1.5, portion x 1 to 1.4 = 165 to 290 kcal. Basis: USDA's paratha (FNDDS 51300185) weighs 70 g against about 49 g here, so the size factor runs to 1.4. Dhabas often finish parathas with extra ghee or butter; the fat factor of up to 1.5 is a stated assumption.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: paratha: 150 kcal per a paratha, ours 166 (-9%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Bread, paratha (51300185): 326 kcal per 100 g, ours 336 (-3%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707715/nutrients

Judgement calls:

- Fat 2 tbsp = 27.2 g (USDA tablespoon of oil, 13.6 g); all of it counted as eaten. The textbook's oil is sunflower; IFCT's oils all use the same fat factor, so the type doesn't change the calories.
- Water 4 tbsp is INDB's unit for 'enough to make a soft dough'; it has no calories.
- Outside size factor from FNDDS paratha (70 g) against about 49 g here; the fat factor of 1.5 is an assumption.

Sources:

| Wheat flour, atta | 80 | 256.2 | 8.5 | 51.3 | 1.2 | IFCT 2017 A019 |

No cooked weight used (no reliable cooked weight for a stuffed paratha). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 aloo paratha: 0.3333 of the recipe = 190 kcal, P 3 g, C 22 g, F 10 g
- 2 aloo parathas: 0.6667 of the recipe = 380 kcal, P 7 g, C 44 g, F 19 g

Judgement calls:

- No independent analysed value for aloo paratha was found in IFCT 2017, NIN's Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS or a literature search (Europe PMC). INDB's own figure (191 kcal per paratha) is from this same textbook recipe, so it agrees by design.
- Coriander 'a few' sprigs taken as 2 g. Spices under 1 g left out.
- All 2 tbsp of oil (27.2 g) counted as eaten.
- Curd from USDA FDC 171284 (whole-milk yogurt), since IFCT 2017 has no curd.

| Coriander seeds (dhania) | 1 | 2.7 | 0.1 | 0.1 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 G024 |
| Groundnut oil | 13.6 | 120.3 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 13.6 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Groundnut oil | 40.8 | 360.8 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 40.8 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **1337.2** | **33.4** | **153.3** | **64.3** | |

No cooked weight used (no published cooked weight for thepla). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 thepla: 0.0909 of the recipe = 120 kcal, P 3 g, C 14 g, F 6 g
- 2 theplas: 0.1818 of the recipe = 245 kcal, P 6 g, C 28 g, F 12 g
- 3 theplas: 0.2727 of the recipe = 365 kcal, P 9 g, C 42 g, F 18 g
- 4 theplas: 0.3636 of the recipe = 485 kcal, P 12 g, C 56 g, F 23 g
- 2 theplas with 100 g curd: 0.1818 of the recipe + 100 g Curd (dahi), whole milk = 305 kcal, P 10 g, C 33 g, F 15 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- 2 theplas and a cup of chai: thepla:two+masala-chai:cup2 = 305 kcal, P 8 g, C 39 g, F 13 g

Outside (Farsan shop or packed): fat sources x 1 to 1.5, portion x 1 to 1.2 = 120 to 170 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for shop or packed theplas was found. A size of up to 1.2 times and up to 1.5 times the oil are stated assumptions; shop theplas are often softer and oilier so they keep.

Judgement calls:

- No textbook thepla exists in INDB; this is its open-source recipe OSR104. INDB's serving count of 11 is marked as guessed; kept, since it gives about 25 g of flour per thepla.
- No independent analysed value for thepla was found (IFCT 2017, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS, Europe PMC).
- Besan valued as IFCT Bengal gram dal (B001), since IFCT 2017 has no separate besan entry.
- Ginger '1 inch' taken as 10 g fresh ginger (INDB mapped it to ground ginger at 12.66 g). Green chilli 0.75 tsp chopped taken as 3 g; curd 4.5 tbsp as 67.5 g.
- All 4 tbsp of oil (54.4 g) counted as eaten.

Sources:

## Puri (/calories/puri/)

Recipe: 80 g atta with a teaspoon of ghee, deep-fried into 6 puris.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, atta | 80 | 256.2 | 8.5 | 51.3 | 1.2 | IFCT 2017 A019 |
| Ghee | 5 | 44.2 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 5.0 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| Water | 60 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Groundnut oil | 31 | 274.1 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 31.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **574.6** | **8.5** | **51.3** | **37.2** | |

Cooked yield 149 g (the atta's non-fat solids (69.9 g) at USDA's puri composition, 28% water and 24.9% fat, so 69.9 / 0.4706). Per 100 g cooked: 385.6 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 puri (about 13 g atta): 25 g cooked = 95 kcal, P 1 g, C 9 g, F 6 g
- 1 large puri: 36 g cooked = 140 kcal, P 2 g, C 12 g, F 9 g
- 3 puris: 75 g cooked = 285 kcal, P 4 g, C 26 g, F 19 g
- 4 puris: 99 g cooked = 385 kcal, P 6 g, C 34 g, F 25 g
- 6 puris: 149 g cooked = 575 kcal, P 8 g, C 51 g, F 37 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- 4 puris with aloo sabzi: puri:four+aloo-sabzi:k200 = 570 kcal, P 9 g, C 55 g, F 35 g

Outside (Halwai or puri-bhaji stall): fat sources x 1 to 1.22, portion x 1 to 1.45 = 95 to 155 kcal. Basis: Size up to USDA's 36 g puri (FNDDS 51300180) against about 25 g here, so 1.45. Fat up to 1.22 times: Jain et al. 2024 found total fat in Indian fried foods (including puri) rose from a mean of 22.5% to 27.4% of dry weight between the 1st and 32nd frying cycle in the same oil.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: puri: 80 kcal per a puri, ours 96 (-16%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Bread, puri (51300180): 409 kcal per 100 g, ours 386 (+6%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707714/nutrients

Range shown on page: NIN's 80 kcal per puri is 16% below this page's home puri. The difference is puri size and the oil-uptake assumption. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Oil taken up: solved so the puri matches USDA's puri composition (FNDDS 51300180: 28% water, 24.9% fat). The atta's non-fat solids are 69.9 g, so fat = 69.9 x 24.94 / 47.06 = 37.0 g; minus atta fat (1.2 g) and the dough ghee (5 g) leaves about 31 g of oil for 6 puris.
- The textbook makes 5 to 6 puris from 80 g atta; 6 used here.
- INDB counts 480 ml of frying oil in the recipe (738 kcal per 100 g), so its values aren't used.
- Jain et al. 2024 report fat on a dry-weight basis; only the ratio between the 1st and 32nd frying (27.4 / 22.5 = 1.22) is used.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, FNDDS Bread, puri (2707714). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707714/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Jain, Passi and Selvamurthy, Total fat and fatty acid profile of Indian fried foods, Journal of Food Science and Technology, 2024. https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC11465016 (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Bhatura (/calories/bhatura/)

Recipe: 80 g maida and 20 g curd, deep-fried into 3 bhaturas.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 80 | 281.4 | 8.3 | 59.4 | 0.6 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Curd (dahi), whole milk | 20 | 12.2 | 0.7 | 0.9 | 0.7 | USDA FDC 171284 |
| Sugar, white | 1 | 3.9 | 0.0 | 1.0 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| Water | 25 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Groundnut oil | 29 | 256.4 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 29.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **554.0** | **9.0** | **61.3** | **30.3** | |

No cooked weight used (no measured cooked weight for a home bhatura; the raw dough plus oil is about 52 g each). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- Half a bhatura: 0.1667 of the recipe = 90 kcal, P 1 g, C 10 g, F 5 g
- 1 bhatura (about 27 g maida): 0.3333 of the recipe = 185 kcal, P 3 g, C 20 g, F 10 g
- 2 bhaturas: 0.6667 of the recipe = 370 kcal, P 6 g, C 41 g, F 20 g
- 3 bhaturas: 1 of the recipe = 555 kcal, P 9 g, C 61 g, F 30 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- 2 bhature with chole: bhatura:two+chole:k200 = 635 kcal, P 15 g, C 67 g, F 32 g

Outside (Street stall (measured)): fat sources x 0.47 to 0.98, portion x 0.64 to 1.01 = 90 to 185 kcal. Basis: Calibrated to Gupta et al. 2016, which bought and analysed bhaturas from vendors: a village bhatura of 33.00 g with 3.30 g fat, and a Delhi urban slum bhatura of 52.04 g with 10.20 g fat. Size factor = vendor weight against about 51.7 g here; fat factor chosen so total fat matches the measured fat.

Judgement calls:

- Home oil uptake set so fat is 19.6% of the raw dough plus oil, from Gupta et al. 2016's urban bhatura (10.20 g fat in 52.04 g). (1.26 + x) / (126 + x) = 0.196 gives x = 29 g for 3 bhaturas. Moisture lost in frying is not counted.
- The resulting bhatura (about 51.7 g raw dough plus oil) is close to the 52 g Delhi bhatura, so 3 bhaturas from 80 g maida fits.
- Water in the dough (25 g) is an assumption; it has no calories and only affects weight.
- No second analysed source for bhatura energy was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS, Europe PMC). INDB counts 480 ml of frying oil (793 kcal per 100 g), so its values aren't used.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Gupta et al., Unhealthy fat in street and snack foods, Journal of Nutrition Education and Behavior, 2016. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4826272 (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Yogurt, plain, whole milk (171284). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/171284/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Sugars, granulated (169655). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169655/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Naan (/calories/naan/)

Recipe: 80 g maida with curd, milk and a little sugar, making 2 naans.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 80 | 281.4 | 8.3 | 59.4 | 0.6 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Curd (dahi), whole milk | 25 | 15.3 | 0.9 | 1.2 | 0.8 | USDA FDC 171284 |
| Milk, whole, cow | 20 | 14.6 | 0.7 | 1.0 | 0.9 | IFCT 2017 L002 |
| Sugar, white | 4.2 | 16.3 | 0.0 | 4.2 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| Ghee | 2.5 | 22.1 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 2.5 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| **Total** | | **349.6** | **9.8** | **65.8** | **4.8** | |

Cooked yield 118 g (the dough's solids (83.3 g) at the 29.2% water of USDA's naan, so 83.3 / 0.708). Per 100 g cooked: 296.3 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 home naan (40 g maida): 59 g cooked = 175 kcal, P 5 g, C 33 g, F 2 g
- ¼ of a large restaurant naan: 44 g cooked = 130 kcal, P 4 g, C 25 g, F 2 g
- 1 large restaurant naan (10 inch): 177 g cooked = 525 kcal, P 15 g, C 99 g, F 7 g
- 100 g naan: 100 g cooked = 295 kcal, P 8 g, C 56 g, F 4 g

Outside (Restaurant (tandoor)): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 2 to 3 = 350 to 525 kcal. Basis: USDA FNDDS lists a 10-inch naan at 177 g, three times the 59 g home naan here; the lower end of 2 times is a stated assumption for smaller restaurant naans.

Cross-checks:

- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Bread, naan (51108100): 311 kcal per 100 g, ours 296 (+5%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707613/nutrients
- USDA SR Legacy, Bread, naan, plain, commercially prepared (171845): 291 kcal per 100 g, ours 296 (-2%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/171845/nutrients

Judgement calls:

- INDB records the maida as 100 g though the textbook says 80 g; 80 g used here.
- The textbook's 1 tsp butter is taken as brushing and moved to the butter naan page. Half a teaspoon of melted fat taken as 2.5 g ghee.
- Cooked yield from water content: solids = maida 70.9 + milk 2.7 + curd 3.0 + sugar 4.2 + ghee 2.5 = 83.3 g; at 29.24% water (FNDDS naan) that's 118 g.
- Restaurant sizes from FNDDS portions: a 10-inch naan 177 g, a quarter 44 g.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Butter naan (/calories/butter-naan/)

Recipe: 80 g maida with curd, milk and a little sugar, making 2 naans.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 80 | 281.4 | 8.3 | 59.4 | 0.6 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Curd (dahi), whole milk | 25 | 15.3 | 0.9 | 1.2 | 0.8 | USDA FDC 171284 |
| Milk, whole, cow | 20 | 14.6 | 0.7 | 1.0 | 0.9 | IFCT 2017 L002 |
| Sugar, white | 4.2 | 16.3 | 0.0 | 4.2 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| Ghee | 2.5 | 22.1 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 2.5 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| **Total** | | **349.6** | **9.8** | **65.8** | **4.8** | |

Cooked yield 118 g (the dough's solids (83.3 g) at the 29.2% water of USDA's naan, as on the naan page). Per 100 g cooked: 296.3 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 home naan, ½ tsp butter (2.5 g): 59 g cooked + 2.5 g Butter = 195 kcal, P 5 g, C 33 g, F 4 g
- 1 home naan, 1 tsp butter (5 g): 59 g cooked + 5 g Butter = 210 kcal, P 5 g, C 33 g, F 6 g
- 1 home naan, 2 tsp butter (10 g): 59 g cooked + 10 g Butter = 245 kcal, P 5 g, C 33 g, F 11 g
- 1 large restaurant naan, 2 tsp butter: 177 g cooked + 10 g Butter = 595 kcal, P 15 g, C 99 g, F 15 g
- Half a large naan, 1 tsp butter: 89 g cooked + 5 g Butter = 300 kcal, P 7 g, C 49 g, F 8 g

Outside (Restaurant or delivery): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 2 to 3 = 420 to 630 kcal. Basis: USDA FNDDS lists a 10-inch naan at 177 g, three times the 59 g home naan here; 2 times is a stated assumption for smaller restaurant naans. Butter is assumed to scale with the naan's size, so 2 to 3 teaspoons.

Judgement calls:

- Same naan recipe and yield as the naan page (ASC142, 80 g maida, 2 naans).
- Butter amounts per naan (½, 1 and 2 tsp) are a typical range; the textbook's 1 tsp for 2 naans is the ½ tsp row. Teaspoon of butter taken as 5 g (USDA pat, 5 g).
- No analysed value for butter naan was found, so there is no independent cross-check beyond the plain naan page's USDA checks.
- FAQ figures for two naans: 2 x 210.8 = 421.6 kcal (home, 1 tsp each); 2 x 524.6 + 2 x 71.7 = 1,193 kcal for two large naans with 2 tsp butter each.

Sources:

Cross-checks:

- Fenugreek 0.25 tsp taken as 1 g.

Cooked yield 228 g (the ingredients added together, with no allowance for water boiled off, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 89.4 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (dal taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 135 kcal, P 5 g, C 13 g, F 7 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 180 kcal, P 6 g, C 17 g, F 9 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 250 g cooked = 225 kcal, P 8 g, C 21 g, F 12 g
- Half a cup (100 ml): 100 g cooked = 90 kcal, P 3 g, C 8 g, F 5 g
- 1 bowl (30 g dry dal): 228 g cooked = 205 kcal, P 7 g, C 19 g, F 11 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- Dal fry with rice (a katori of each): dal-fry:k200+plain-rice:k200 = 340 kcal, P 10 g, C 52 g, F 10 g
- Dal fry with 2 rotis: dal-fry:k200+2*roti:medium = 370 kcal, P 13 g, C 55 g, F 10 g

Outside (Dhaba or restaurant): fat sources x 1 to 1.5, portion x 1 to 1.5 = 180 to 325 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for restaurant dal fry was found. Up to 1.5 times the dal per katori (thicker) and up to 1.5 times the ghee are stated assumptions, in line with the dal tadka page.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: plain dal, half a cup: 100 kcal per half a cup, ours 89 (+12%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Dal (41305050): 145 kcal per 100 g, ours 89 (+62%). A generic survey recipe, thicker than this one. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707427/nutrients

Range shown on page: USDA FNDDS 'Dal' (41305050, 145 kcal per 100 g) is 62% above this page's dal fry; NIN's plain dal is within 12%. The difference is thickness. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Composite recipe from three Basic Food Preparation components (via INDB): arhar dal BFP172 (30 g dal), onion-tomato baghar BFP168 and cumin baghar BFP167.
- Water 150 ml (half of BFP172's 300 ml) is an assumption for a thicker dal fry.
- Yield = sum of ingredients (no evaporation), following INDB. Density 1 g per ml assumed.
- Range top: 1.6 times this katori (about 290 kcal) brings it close to FNDDS dal at 145 kcal per 100 g.

Cooked yield 271 g (the ingredients added together, with no allowance for water boiled off, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 37.4 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (thin dal taken as 1 g per ml, like water; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 bowl (30 g dry dal, about 270 ml): 271 g cooked = 100 kcal, P 7 g, C 17 g, F 1 g
- 1 bowl with 1 tsp ghee (5 g): 271 g cooked + 5 g Ghee = 145 kcal, P 7 g, C 17 g, F 6 g

Judgement calls:

- Bowl = NIN's 30 g pulse portion. NIN's 'plain dhal, half cup, 100 kcal' is compared with this bowl because both hold one portion of dry dal; NIN's dal is thicker, so the volume differs.

Cooked yield 229 g (the ingredients added together, with no allowance for water boiled off, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 90.0 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (dal taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 135 kcal, P 5 g, C 12 g, F 7 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 180 kcal, P 7 g, C 17 g, F 10 g

Basis: The Indian Nutrient Databank's open-source chana dal (OSR142) uses 3 tablespoons of ghee for 200 g of dal, about 0.19 g of fat per g of dal against 0.30 g here; restaurant versions vary widely, so up to twice this page's fat is a stated assumption.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: plain dal, half a cup: 100 kcal per half a cup, ours 90 (+11%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Judgement calls:

- ASC153 without its 50 g of lauki, as the dal tadka page removed spinach. Lauki is about 11 kcal per 100 g (IFCT D007), so it changes the bowl by about 5 kcal.
- Fat 2 tsp = 9 g (USDA teaspoon of oil, 4.5 g).
- Spices under 1 g (garam masala, chilli powder) left out.

| Besan (ground Bengal gram dal) | 20 | 65.8 | 4.3 | 9.3 | 1.1 | IFCT 2017 B001 |
| Water | 400 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Groundnut oil | 13.6 | 120.3 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 13.6 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Red chillies, dry, whole | 6.7 | 15.9 | 0.9 | 2.0 | 0.4 | IFCT 2017 G022 |
| Turmeric powder | 2.25 | 6.3 | 0.2 | 1.1 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 G033 |

Cooked yield 546 g (the ingredients added together, with no allowance for water boiled off, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 50.4 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (kadhi taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 75 kcal, P 3 g, C 5 g, F 5 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 100 kcal, P 3 g, C 6 g, F 7 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 250 g cooked = 125 kcal, P 4 g, C 8 g, F 9 g
- 1 bowl, half the recipe: 273 g cooked = 140 kcal, P 5 g, C 9 g, F 9 g
- 1 bowl with its share of pakoras: 0.5 of the recipe + 15 g Besan (ground Bengal gram dal) + 10 g Spinach (palak) + 7.5 g Onion, big + 18.7 g Groundnut oil = 360 kcal, P 8 g, C 17 g, F 29 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- Kadhi with rice (a katori of each): kadhi:k200+plain-rice:k200 = 260 kcal, P 7 g, C 42 g, F 7 g
- Kadhi with 2 rotis: kadhi:k200+2*roti:medium = 295 kcal, P 10 g, C 45 g, F 8 g

Outside (Dhaba kadhi pakora): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.3 = 360 to 465 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for restaurant kadhi was found. The pakoras here already use the fat content of street pakoras measured by Gupta et al. 2016 (30.6%); a serving up to 1.3 times as big is a stated assumption.

Judgement calls:

- ASC168 lists besan twice (20 g and 30 g). The 20 g is taken as the kadhi and the 30 g, with spinach and onion, as the pakoras.
- Pakora oil: fat set to 30.6% of the raw batter (besan 30 + spinach 20 + onion 15 + about 25 g water) plus oil, from Gupta et al. 2016's urban pakora (16.57 g fat in 54.16 g). (1.6 + x) / (90 + x) = 0.306 gives x = 37.4 g for the batch, 18.7 g per bowl.
- No independent analysed value for kadhi was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS, Europe PMC). INDB counts 480 ml of frying oil (2,367 kcal per bowl), so its values aren't used.
- Asafoetida, cumin and coriander seeds (0.25 tsp each) left out.

Sources:

Cooked yield 515 g (the ingredients added together, with no allowance for water boiled off, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 100.5 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (sambar taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 150 kcal, P 5 g, C 16 g, F 7 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 7 g, C 22 g, F 9 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 250 g cooked = 250 kcal, P 9 g, C 27 g, F 11 g
- 1 cup (200 ml), NIN's measure: 200 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 7 g, C 22 g, F 9 g
- 1 bowl, half the recipe: 258 g cooked = 260 kcal, P 9 g, C 28 g, F 12 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 100 kcal, P 4 g, C 11 g, F 5 g

Outside (Udupi restaurant or canteen): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 0.6 to 1 = 120 to 200 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for restaurant sambar was found. Restaurant and canteen sambar served with idli and dosa is usually thinner than this textbook sambar; 0.6 to 1 times is a stated assumption, with the low end near NIN's 110 kcal per cup.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: sambar, 1 cup: 110 kcal per a cup, ours 201 (-45%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Sambar, vegetable stew (41311020): 86 kcal per 100 g, ours 100 (-14%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707430/nutrients

Range shown on page: NIN's 110 kcal per cup is 45% below this page's thick, coconut sambar (ASC167). USDA FNDDS sambar (86 kcal per 100 g) sits in between. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Teaspoon of chana or urad dal taken as 4 g. Coriander seed 1 tsp = 1.8 g; turmeric 0.25 tsp = 0.75 g.
- Asafoetida, mustard seeds and curry leaves (under 1 g each) left out.
- Thin sambar at about 55 kcal per 100 g is NIN's 110 kcal per 200 ml cup.
- Cup = 200 ml (NIN).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| **Total** | | **326.1** | **9.7** | **57.5** | **5.8** | |

Cooked yield 239 g (the recipe's solids (78.5 g) at the 67.2% water of USDA's rice with lentils, so 78.5 / 0.328). Per 100 g cooked: 136.5 kcal. Katori density 0.875 g per ml (USDA's rice with lentils: 207 g per cup (236.6 ml)).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 131 g cooked = 180 kcal, P 5 g, C 32 g, F 3 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 175 g cooked = 240 kcal, P 7 g, C 42 g, F 4 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 219 g cooked = 300 kcal, P 9 g, C 53 g, F 5 g
- 1 plate (the whole recipe): 239 g cooked = 325 kcal, P 10 g, C 57 g, F 6 g
- 200 ml katori, 1 tsp ghee on top: 175 g cooked + 5 g Ghee = 285 kcal, P 7 g, C 42 g, F 9 g
- 200 ml katori with 100 g curd: undefined of the recipe + 100 g Curd (dahi), whole milk = 300 kcal, P 11 g, C 47 g, F 8 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 135 kcal, P 4 g, C 24 g, F 2 g

Outside (Restaurant or thali): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 5 to 10 g Ghee = 285 to 325 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for restaurant khichdi was found. Restaurant and thali khichdi is usually served with ghee poured over; 1 to 2 NIN teaspoons per katori is a stated assumption.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: khichdi, 1 cup: 200 kcal per a 200 ml katori, ours 239 (-16%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Range shown on page: NIN's 200 kcal per cup is 16% below this page's katori; a wetter khichdi explains it. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Yield from water content rather than INDB's sum of ingredients (572 g), which ignores evaporation and absorption: solids = rice 53.9 + moong 18.0 + ginger 0.6 + cumin 0.9 + ghee 5 = 78.5 g; at FNDDS 58160805's 67.2% water, 239 g.
- Density 0.875 g per ml from FNDDS 58160805 (207 g per cup of 236.6 ml).
- Rice is parboiled (A014), as INDB maps 'rice' in this recipe.

| Butter | 21.3 | 152.7 | 0.2 | 0.0 | 17.3 | USDA FDC 173430 |

| Sugar, white | 2.1 | 8.1 | 0.0 | 2.1 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| **Total** | | **700.7** | **58.4** | **16.3** | **44.8** | |

Cooked yield 537 g (the ingredients added together, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does; the textbook adds no water). Per 100 g cooked: 130.5 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 195 kcal, P 16 g, C 5 g, F 13 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 260 kcal, P 22 g, C 6 g, F 17 g
- 1 serving (125 g chicken): 269 g cooked = 350 kcal, P 29 g, C 8 g, F 22 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 130 kcal, P 11 g, C 3 g, F 8 g

Outside (Restaurant or delivery): fat sources x 1.5 to 2, portion x 1 to 1.5 = 400 to 675 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for restaurant butter chicken was found. Up to twice the butter and cream and a serving up to 1.5 times as big are stated assumptions, in line with the restaurant range on the paneer butter masala page.

Judgement calls:

- IFCT 2017's skinless chicken breast is 9.0 g fat per 100 g (168 kcal); USDA's raw chicken meat is 3.1 g (119 kcal, FDC 171052). IFCT is used, as the databank does, so these chicken figures sit at the higher end.
- Ghee/butter 1.5 tbsp taken as butter (21.3 g, USDA 14.2 g per tbsp). Cream 1.5 tbsp = 22.5 g of USDA light cream (19% fat). Tomato puree 2 tbsp = 31 g (USDA 250 g per cup).
- Garam masala, cardamom and coriander left out.
- No analysed value for butter chicken was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS). NIN's chicken curry is a different dish and isn't used as a check.
- FAQ meal: restaurant serving (about 400 to 675) plus a large restaurant butter naan (about 595) passes 1,000 kcal.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

- USDA FoodData Central, Cream, fluid, light (170857). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/170857/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

| Egg, hen, whole, raw | 50 | 67.4 | 6.6 | 0.0 | 4.6 | IFCT 2017 M001 |
| Tomato, ripe, hybrid | 50 | 9.4 | 0.4 | 1.6 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 D075 |
| Onion, big | 30 | 14.4 | 0.4 | 2.9 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 G017 |
| Ginger, fresh | 3 | 1.6 | 0.1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G014 |
| Garlic | 1.25 | 1.5 | 0.1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G011 |
| Chillies, green | 3.3 | 1.4 | 0.1 | 0.2 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G008 |
| Coriander leaves | 1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G009 |
| Coriander seeds (dhania) | 1.8 | 4.8 | 0.2 | 0.2 | 0.3 | IFCT 2017 G024 |

| Groundnut oil | 9 | 79.6 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 9.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **182.7** | **8.0** | **5.8** | **14.2** | |

Cooked yield 150 g (the ingredients added together, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does). Per 100 g cooked: 121.8 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 egg with gravy: 150 g cooked = 185 kcal, P 8 g, C 6 g, F 14 g
- 2 eggs with gravy: 300 g cooked = 365 kcal, P 16 g, C 12 g, F 28 g
- 3 eggs with gravy: 450 g cooked = 550 kcal, P 24 g, C 17 g, F 43 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 120 kcal, P 5 g, C 4 g, F 9 g

Outside (Dhaba or restaurant): fat sources x 1.5 to 2, portion x 1 to 1 = 445 to 525 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for restaurant egg curry was found. Dhabas often fry the boiled eggs before adding them and use more oil in the masala; 1.5 to 2 times this recipe's oil is a stated assumption.

Judgement calls:

- Egg 50 g edible (INDB). IFCT raw whole egg 134.8 kcal per 100 g, so 67 kcal per egg. NIN's 90 kcal boiled egg implies about 61 g at IFCT's boiled value (147.7 kcal per 100 g).
- No analysed value for egg curry was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS).
- Bay leaf, garam masala, chilli powder and cumin (under 1 g each) left out.
- Changes list: one egg about 70 kcal (67) and 7 g protein (6.6); coconut milk 60 g = 138 kcal (USDA 170172).

- 1 katori (200 ml): 134 g cooked = 185 kcal, P 3 g, C 32 g, F 5 g

- 1 katori (200 ml): 145 g cooked = 190 kcal, P 3 g, C 20 g, F 11 g

- Saffron, whole spices and bay leaf left out.
- Raita in the changes list: 100 g curd (61 kcal, USDA) to 150 g (92 kcal).

USDA's 'biryani with meat' is 6.8% fat against 4.8% here; restaurant biryanis are commonly made with much more ghee. 1.5 to 3 times this recipe's fat, in a plate up to 1.5 times as big, is a stated assumption.

IFCT 2017's skinless chicken breast is 9.0 g fat per 100 g (168 kcal); USDA's raw chicken meat is 3.1 g (119 kcal, FDC 171052). - Boiled egg in the changes list: 50 g at IFCT's boiled value is 74 kcal.

## Curd rice (/calories/curd-rice/)

Recipe: one plate, 80 g rice with 60 g curd, 60 ml milk and a 1 teaspoon tadka.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rice, parboiled, milled | 80 | 281.3 | 6.2 | 61.7 | 0.4 | IFCT 2017 A014 |
| Water | 158.4 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Curd (dahi), whole milk | 61.25 | 37.4 | 2.1 | 2.9 | 2.0 | USDA FDC 171284 |
| Milk, whole, cow | 60 | 43.7 | 2.0 | 3.0 | 2.7 | IFCT 2017 L002 |
| Chillies, green | 3.3 | 1.4 | 0.1 | 0.2 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G008 |
| Red chillies, dry, whole | 3.3 | 7.8 | 0.4 | 1.0 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 G022 |
| Ginger, fresh | 1 | 0.6 | 0.0 | 0.1 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G014 |
| Groundnut oil | 4.5 | 39.8 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 4.5 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **411.9** | **10.8** | **68.8** | **9.9** | |

Cooked yield 372 g (rice at 2.98 times its raw weight (Bognár 2002), plus the other ingredients). Per 100 g cooked: 110.7 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (mashed curd rice taken as 1 g per ml; a stated assumption).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 150 g cooked = 165 kcal, P 4 g, C 28 g, F 4 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 220 kcal, P 6 g, C 37 g, F 5 g
- 1 katori (250 ml): 250 g cooked = 275 kcal, P 7 g, C 46 g, F 7 g
- 1 plate (80 g raw rice): 372 g cooked = 410 kcal, P 11 g, C 69 g, F 10 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 110 kcal, P 3 g, C 18 g, F 3 g

Outside (Restaurant or meals hotel): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.3, plus 0 to 5 g Ghee = 220 to 330 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for restaurant curd rice was found. A katori up to 1.3 times as dense and up to a teaspoon of ghee in the tadka are stated assumptions.

Judgement calls:

- INDB maps the rice to parboiled (A014); kept. Curd 0.25 cup = 61.25 g (INDB); milk 0.25 cup = 60 g.
- Mustard seeds, curry leaves and asafoetida left out (under 1 g each).
- No independent analysed value for curd rice was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS).
- Without tadka: 4.5 g oil = 40 kcal.

Sources:

No cooked weight used (no cooked weight used; each chilla is a sixth of the batter). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 chilla, no oil (non-stick): 0.1667 of the recipe = 60 kcal, P 4 g, C 9 g, F 1 g
- 1 chilla, ½ tsp oil: 0.1667 of the recipe + 2.25 g Groundnut oil = 80 kcal, P 4 g, C 9 g, F 3 g
- 1 chilla, 1 tsp oil: 0.1667 of the recipe + 4.5 g Groundnut oil = 100 kcal, P 4 g, C 9 g, F 5 g
- 2 chillas, ½ tsp oil each: 0.3333 of the recipe + 4.5 g Groundnut oil = 160 kcal, P 8 g, C 17 g, F 6 g
- 1 chilla stuffed with 20 g paneer: 0.1667 of the recipe + 2.25 g Groundnut oil + 20 g Paneer = 130 kcal, P 8 g, C 11 g, F 6 g

Outside (Cafe or breakfast stall): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.3, plus 4.5 to 9 g Groundnut oil = 200 to 290 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for besan chilla from a cafe or stall was found. Chillas up to 1.3 times as big with 1 to 2 extra teaspoons of oil for two are stated assumptions.

Judgement calls:

- Open-source recipe OSR100 (INDB). INDB's serving count of 6 is marked as guessed; kept, so 16.7 g besan per chilla.
- Besan valued as IFCT Bengal gram dal (B001).
- Ginger '0.5 inch' taken as 6.3 g fresh ginger (INDB mapped it to ground ginger). Turmeric '2 to 3 pinches' = 0.625 tsp = 1.9 g. Ajwain (0.5 tsp) left out.
- No independent analysed value for besan chilla was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS). INDB's per-chilla value (70.8 kcal) is from this recipe with its 0.75 tsp of oil.
- Paneer in the changes list: 20 g x 258 kcal per 100 g = 52 kcal.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

| **Total** | | **634.2** | **37.9** | **78.0** | **17.7** | |

Cooked yield 316 g (the ingredients added together, as the Indian Nutrient Databank does; steaming keeps the water in). Per 100 g cooked: 200.7 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 piece (about 40 g): 40 g cooked = 80 kcal, P 5 g, C 10 g, F 2 g
- 2 pieces (textbook serving): 79 g cooked = 160 kcal, P 9 g, C 19 g, F 4 g
- 4 pieces: 158 g cooked = 315 kcal, P 19 g, C 39 g, F 9 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 12 g, C 25 g, F 6 g

Outside (Farsan shop khaman): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 4.5 to 9 g Groundnut oil, plus 4.2 to 8.4 g Sugar, white = 215 to 270 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for shop khaman was found. Farsan shops pour a tempering of oil, sugar and water over khaman; half to one teaspoon each of oil and sugar per piece is a stated assumption.

Cross-checks:

- Indian Nutrient Databank, Khaman (BFP439), per piece: 64.9 kcal per a piece, ours 79 (-18%). Basic Food Preparation's khaman from soaked chana dal, 6 pieces. https://github.com/lindsayjaacks/Indian-Nutrient-Databank-INDB-

Range shown on page: INDB's BFP439 khaman (64.9 kcal per piece) is 18% below this page's piece; piece size differs. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Besan valued as IFCT Bengal gram dal (B001). Soda (0.5 tsp) has no calories.
- Range top of about 100 kcal per piece: 79 + 0.5 tsp oil (20) = about 100.
- Chutney in the changes list: NIN tamarind chutney with jaggery, 60 kcal per tbsp.

## Medu vada (/calories/medu-vada/)

Recipe: 60 g urad dal, ground and deep-fried into 4 vadas.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Black gram dal (urad) | 60 | 194.5 | 13.8 | 30.6 | 1.0 | IFCT 2017 B003 |
| Water | 15 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Groundnut oil | 9.8 | 86.7 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 9.8 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **281.1** | **13.8** | **30.6** | **10.8** | |

Cooked yield 111 g (the dal's non-fat solids (53.5 g) at USDA's vada composition, 42% water and 9.7% fat, so 53.5 / 0.4827). Per 100 g cooked: 253.3 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 medu vada: 28 g cooked = 70 kcal, P 3 g, C 8 g, F 3 g
- 2 medu vadas: 56 g cooked = 140 kcal, P 7 g, C 15 g, F 5 g
- 4 medu vadas: 111 g cooked = 280 kcal, P 14 g, C 31 g, F 11 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 255 kcal, P 12 g, C 28 g, F 10 g

Meals (sums of the page values of each dish's portion, calories rounded to 5):

- 2 medu vadas with sambar: medu-vada:two+sambar:k200 = 340 kcal, P 14 g, C 37 g, F 15 g

Outside (Udupi restaurant or stall): fat sources x 1 to 1.22, portion x 1 to 1.5 = 140 to 225 kcal. Basis: Fat up to 1.22 times: Jain et al. 2024 found total fat in Indian fried foods rose from a mean of 22.5% to 27.4% of dry weight between the 1st and 32nd frying in the same oil. Vadas up to 1.5 times as big are a stated assumption.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: vada, 2: 140 kcal per 2 vadas, ours 141 (0%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Vada (58175000): 266 kcal per 100 g, ours 253 (+5%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2709130/nutrients

Judgement calls:

- Oil taken up: solved so the vada matches USDA's vada composition (FNDDS 58175000: 42.0% water, 9.73% fat). Urad dal non-fat solids = 60 x (1 - 0.0916 - 0.0169) = 53.5 g; fat = 53.5 x 9.73 / 48.27 = 10.8 g; minus the dal's own 1.0 g leaves 9.8 g of oil for 4 vadas.
- Cooked vada about 27.7 g, close to FNDDS's 30 g per vada.
- INDB counts 240 ml of frying oil (746 kcal per 100 g), so its values aren't used.

A vada pav up to 1.3 times as big is a stated assumption.

Judgement calls:

- Oil taken up: set so the textbook's 4 bondas are 100 kcal each, ICMR-NIN's potato bonda (2 for 200 kcal). Before frying the recipe is 221.5 kcal, so (400 - 221.5) / 8.843 = 20.2 g of oil. Because NIN is used to set the oil, it isn't listed as a separate check.
- Besan 2 tbsp = 15 g; rice flour 2 tsp = 5 g (USDA). Cashew 2 nuts = 2.5 g (INDB).
- Pav 43 g: INDB's OSR112 pav bhaji lists 12 pav at 516 g. Valued as USDA FDC 172793, dinner rolls.
- Chana and urad dal (0.125 tsp each), mustard seeds, curry leaves, coriander and soda left out.
- Cheese slice in the changes list: 20 g at about 300 to 340 kcal per 100 g.

No cooked weight used (no cooked weight used; a plate of bhaji is a fifth of the recipe). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 plate of bhaji, no pav: 0.2 of the recipe = 215 kcal, P 6 g, C 18 g, F 12 g
- Bhaji with 1 butter pav: 0.2 of the recipe + 43 g Pav (soft bread roll) + 5 g Butter = 385 kcal, P 10 g, C 40 g, F 19 g
- Bhaji with 2 butter pav: 0.2 of the recipe + 86 g Pav (soft bread roll) + 10 g Butter = 550 kcal, P 15 g, C 61 g, F 26 g
- Bhaji with 4 butter pav: 0.2 of the recipe + 172 g Pav (soft bread roll) + 20 g Butter = 890 kcal, P 25 g, C 104 g, F 40 g
- 1 extra butter pav: 0 of the recipe + 43 g Pav (soft bread roll) + 5 g Butter = 170 kcal, P 5 g, C 22 g, F 7 g

Outside (Street or restaurant): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 10 to 25 g Butter = 625 to 730 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for street pav bhaji was found. Street stalls are known for adding a block of butter to the bhaji and pav; 10 to 25 g more per plate is a stated assumption.

Judgement calls:

- Open-source recipe OSR112 (INDB), 5 servings. Butter: 3 tbsp in the bhaji plus 6 tbsp (85.2 g) of the 5 to 7 tbsp for topping and toasting, split as 5 g per pav (12 pav, 60 g) and 25 g on top of the bhaji.
- Lemon '1 no' taken as 48 g juice (USDA FDC 167747). Ginger-garlic paste 1 tsp each = 5 g. Coriander 3.5 tbsp = 3.5 g (INDB's 0.25 cup = 4 g).
- Left out: black cardamom, cloves, mace, nutmeg, cinnamon, star anise, amchur and dried ginger powder (under 15 kcal together for 5 plates).
- No independent analysed value for pav bhaji was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS). INDB's per-serving value (561 kcal) is from this recipe with hamburger buns as the pav.
- Without butter: bhaji butter (67.6 g, 485 kcal) / 5 = 97 kcal per plate; pav butter 5 g = 36 kcal. Plate with 2 plain pav: bhaji 213 - 97 + 2 x 133 = 382, about 385.

Judgement calls:

- Assembled plate; all amounts other than the shells and chutney are typical amounts, stated as assumptions: potato 30 g, kala chana 4 g dry, onion 10 g, pani 150 ml with 3 g tamarind and 5 g each of mint and coriander.
- Shells: USDA FDC 1913634 (branded label, packaged pani puri kit): 39 g = 5 puris with boondi, 125 kcal, 2 g protein, 18 g carbohydrate (1.01 g fibre), 5 g fat. Carbohydrate taken as 18 - 1.01 g. 6 puris = 46.8 g.
- Sweet chutney as The Art & Science of Cooking's saunth chutney (ASC281: 25 g tamarind and 50 g jaggery in 100 ml water), so 1 tablespoon (15 g) holds 2.1 g tamarind and 4.3 g jaggery, about 21 kcal.
- No independent analysed value for pani puri was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS, Europe PMC).
- Pani alone: tamarind 3 g (8.7 kcal) + mint 5 g (1.9) + coriander 5 g (1.6) = about 12 kcal, about 15 with spices.

Portions:

- Half a plate: 56 g cooked = 130 kcal, P 3 g, C 18 g, F 5 g

Judgement calls:

- Assembled plate. OSR114 (INDB open-source bhel puri) makes its own sev and papdi in bulk and counts all frying oil, so its totals (3,574 kcal per plate) aren't used; its puffed rice (60 g for 2 to 3 plates) and peanuts (2 tbsp) guide the amounts.
- Sev 10 g = besan 6.0 g + oil 4.0 g; papdi 2 x 4 g = maida 4.9 g + oil 3.1 g. Moisture lost in frying isn't counted.
- Amounts of potato (20 g), onion (15 g), tomato (15 g), coriander (5 g) and lemon (3 g) are typical amounts, stated as assumptions.
- No independent analysed value for bhel puri was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS).

| Tomato, ripe, hybrid | 10 | 1.9 | 0.1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 D075 |
| Tamarind pulp | 4.3 | 12.4 | 0.1 | 2.9 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 E064 |
| Jaggery, cane | 8.6 | 30.4 | 0.2 | 7.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 I001 |

| **Total** | | **296.9** | **4.9** | **33.2** | **15.9** | |

No cooked weight used (no cooked weight used; the recipe is one plate of 6). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 sev puri: 0.1667 of the recipe = 50 kcal, P 1 g, C 6 g, F 3 g
- 1 plate (6 pieces): 1 of the recipe = 295 kcal, P 5 g, C 33 g, F 16 g
- 1 plate of dahi sev puri (with 60 g curd): 1 of the recipe + 60 g Curd (dahi), whole milk = 335 kcal, P 7 g, C 36 g, F 18 g

Outside (Street stall): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 0.9 to 1.3 = 265 to 385 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for street sev puri was found. Papdi size and the amount of sev vary by stall; 0.9 to 1.3 times this plate is a stated assumption.

Judgement calls:

- Assembled plate. Papdi 6 x 4 g = maida 14.8 g + oil 9.2 g; sev 15 g = besan 9.1 g + oil 5.9 g. Moisture lost in frying isn't counted.
- Potato 40 g, onion 15 g, tomato 10 g, coriander 5 g and 2 tablespoons of sweet chutney are typical amounts, stated as assumptions.
- No independent analysed value for sev puri was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS).
- Changes list: sev 15 g = 82 kcal; papdi 4 g = 22 kcal; curd 60 g = 37 kcal.
