## Kachori (/calories/kachori/)

Recipe: 100 g maida with 30 g urad dal filling, deep-fried into 7 kachoris.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 100 | 351.8 | 10.4 | 74.3 | 0.8 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Groundnut oil | 20.4 | 180.4 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 20.4 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Black gram dal (urad) | 30 | 97.2 | 6.9 | 15.3 | 0.5 | IFCT 2017 B003 |
| Groundnut oil | 9 | 79.6 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 9.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Lemon juice | 24 | 8.8 | 0.1 | 0.0 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 E033 |
| Red chilli powder | 1.35 | 3.8 | 0.2 | 0.2 | 0.2 | USDA FDC 171319 |
| Fennel seeds (saunf) | 0.5 | 1.7 | 0.1 | 0.1 | 0.1 | USDA FDC 171323 |
| Cumin seeds | 0.5 | 1.5 | 0.1 | 0.1 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 G025 |
| Water | 45 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Groundnut oil | 13.9 | 122.9 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 13.9 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **847.8** | **17.7** | **89.9** | **45.1** | |

No cooked weight used (no measured cooked weight; the raw dough, filling and oil come to about 35 g a kachori). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 khasta kachori (small): 0.1429 of the recipe = 120 kcal, P 3 g, C 13 g, F 6 g
- 2 kachoris: 0.2857 of the recipe = 240 kcal, P 5 g, C 26 g, F 13 g
- 2 kachoris with a 200 ml katori of aloo sabzi: 0.2857 of the recipe + 100 g Potato, brown skin, big + 50 g Tomato, ripe, hybrid + 30 g Onion, big + 9 g Groundnut oil = 415 kcal, P 7 g, C 45 g, F 22 g

Outside (Street or sweet shop (measured)): fat sources x 1.77 to 2.6, portion x 1.59 to 1.54 = 260 to 320 kcal. Basis: Calibrated to Gupta et al. 2016, which bought and analysed kachoris from vendors: a village kachori of 55.70 g with 17.82 g fat, and a Delhi urban slum kachori of 53.83 g with 25.09 g fat. Size factor = vendor weight against about 35 g here; fat factor chosen so total fat matches the measured fat.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: pea kachori, 2: 380 kcal per 2 kachoris, ours 242 (+57%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Range shown on page: NIN's pea kachori (190 kcal each) is 57% above this page's small home kachori; Gupta et al.'s street kachoris are higher again. Size and fat differ. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Home oil uptake: fat set to 18.3% of raw dough, filling and oil (Sakhale et al. 2011, lab-fried samosa pastry). Own fat = 30.9 g; (30.9 + x) / (230.8 + x) = 0.183 gives x = 13.9 g for 7 kachoris. Moisture lost in frying isn't counted.
- Street kachoris (Gupta et al. 2016, Tables 1 and 2): village 55.70 g, 17.82 g fat; urban 53.83 g, 25.09 g fat. Size 55.70 / 34.96 = 1.59 and 53.83 / 34.96 = 1.54; fat factors 1.77 and 2.60 solve for the measured fat.
- Lemon '0.5 lemon' = 24 g juice (USDA). Asafoetida left out. INDB counts 480 ml frying oil (713 kcal per 100 g), so its values aren't used.
- Aloo sabzi row: a 200 ml katori from the aloo sabzi page's recipe, 100 g potato, 50 g tomato, 30 g onion and 9 g oil (spices left out).

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)

- Sakhale et al., oil uptake in samosa, Journal of Food Science and Technology, 2011. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3551041 (accessed 2026-10-11)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 200 | 703.6 | 20.7 | 148.5 | 1.5 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Water | 100 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Cabbage, green | 100 | 21.5 | 1.4 | 3.3 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 C015 |
| Carrot, orange | 100 | 33.2 | 0.9 | 5.5 | 0.5 | IFCT 2017 F002 |
| Onion, big | 25 | 12.0 | 0.4 | 2.4 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 G017 |
| Ginger, fresh | 10 | 5.5 | 0.2 | 0.9 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 G014 |
| Garlic | 1.25 | 1.5 | 0.1 | 0.3 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G011 |
| Chillies, green | 3.3 | 1.4 | 0.1 | 0.2 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G008 |
| Black pepper | 1.2 | 2.6 | 0.1 | 0.4 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G031 |
| Groundnut oil | 13.5 | 119.4 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 13.5 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **900.7** | **23.9** | **161.5** | **15.8** | |

Cooked yield 554 g (the ingredients added together; at 25 g a momo, the size USDA lists for a dumpling, that's about 22 momos). Per 100 g cooked: 162.6 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 momo (25 g): 25 g cooked = 40 kcal, P 1 g, C 7 g, F 1 g
- 1 plate (6 momos): 150 g cooked = 245 kcal, P 6 g, C 44 g, F 4 g
- 1 plate (8 momos): 200 g cooked = 325 kcal, P 9 g, C 58 g, F 6 g
- 10 momos: 250 g cooked = 405 kcal, P 11 g, C 73 g, F 7 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 165 kcal, P 4 g, C 29 g, F 3 g

Outside (Street cart, steamed or fried): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 0 to 11.6 g Groundnut oil = 245 to 345 kcal. Basis: USDA FNDDS lists a fried dumpling at 11.1 g fat per 100 g against 3.4 g for a steamed one with no meat, so frying adds about 7.7 g of fat per 100 g, or 11.6 g for 150 g of momos.

Cross-checks:

- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Dumpling, no meat (55610300): 125 kcal per 100 g, ours 163 (-23%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2708344/nutrients

Range shown on page: USDA FNDDS dumpling, no meat (125 kcal per 100 g) is 23% below this page's momos, which have a thicker maida wrapper. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Recipe: NephroPlus vegetable steamed momos (maida 1 cup = 200 g as stated, carrot 100 g, cabbage 100 g, spring onion 25 g, garlic 2 cloves, ginger 1 inch, green chilli 1, pepper 0.5 tsp, oil 3 tsp). Spring onion valued as onion. Ginger 1 inch = 10 g, as on the thepla page.
- Water 100 ml (half the flour) is an assumption; with it the recipe weighs 554 g, about 22 momos at USDA's 25 g per dumpling.
- Fried range: FNDDS 58111120 fried dumpling, no meat, 11.1 g fat per 100 g; FNDDS 55610300 steamed, 3.37 g. (11.1 - 3.37) x 1.5 = 11.6 g oil for 150 g.
- Range low end: 125 kcal per 100 g x 150 g = 188 kcal.
- Mayonnaise in the changes list: USDA 1 tbsp = 13.8 g (INDB unit table) at about 680 kcal per 100 g, about 94 kcal.

Sources:

## Kuttu puri (/calories/kuttu-puri/)

Recipe: 80 g kuttu atta and 40 g boiled potato, deep-fried into 6 puris.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kuttu (buckwheat) flour | 80 | 268.0 | 10.1 | 48.5 | 2.5 | USDA FDC 170687 |
| Potato, brown skin, big | 40 | 27.9 | 0.6 | 6.0 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 F006 |
| Water | 20 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Groundnut oil | 38 | 336.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 38.0 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **632.0** | **10.7** | **54.4** | **40.6** | |

Cooked yield 163 g (the flour and potato's non-fat solids (76.7 g) at USDA's puri composition, 28% water and 24.9% fat, so 76.7 / 0.4706). Per 100 g cooked: 387.7 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 kuttu puri: 27 g cooked = 105 kcal, P 2 g, C 9 g, F 7 g
- 2 kuttu puris: 54 g cooked = 210 kcal, P 4 g, C 18 g, F 14 g
- 4 kuttu puris: 109 g cooked = 420 kcal, P 7 g, C 36 g, F 27 g
- 4 puris with a 200 ml katori of aloo sabzi: 0.6667 of the recipe + 100 g Potato, brown skin, big + 50 g Tomato, ripe, hybrid + 9 g Ghee = 580 kcal, P 9 g, C 53 g, F 36 g

Outside (Fasting thali or sweet shop): fat sources x 1 to 1.22, portion x 1 to 1.3 = 105 to 155 kcal. Basis: Fat up to 1.22 times: Jain et al. 2024 found total fat in Indian fried foods rose from a mean of 22.5% to 27.4% of dry weight between the 1st and 32nd frying in the same oil. Puris up to 1.3 times as big are a stated assumption.

Judgement calls:

- Adapted recipe: the puri page's ASC107 structure (80 g flour for 6 puris) with kuttu atta (USDA FDC 170687) and 40 g boiled potato as a binder (assumption).
- Oil taken up: non-fat solids = kuttu 80 x (1 - 0.112 - 0.031) = 68.6 g + potato 40 x (1 - 0.795 - 0.002) = 8.1 g = 76.7 g. Fat = 76.7 x 24.94 / 47.06 = 40.6 g; minus own fat 2.6 g = 38 g of oil for 6 puris.
- No analysed value for kuttu puri was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS).
- Aloo sabzi row: a 200 ml katori of fasting aloo sabzi, 100 g potato, 50 g tomato and 9 g ghee (no onion or garlic).

Sources:

## Makhana (/calories/makhana/)

100 g of dry makhana.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Makhana (lotus seeds), dry | 100 | 332.0 | 15.4 | 64.5 | 2.0 | USDA FDC 170149 |
| **Total** | | **332.0** | **15.4** | **64.5** | **2.0** | |

Cooked yield 100 g (dry makhana, not cooked; roasting doesn't change the weight much). Per 100 g cooked: 332.0 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 cup (about 32 g): 32 g cooked = 105 kcal, P 5 g, C 21 g, F 1 g
- 20 g: 20 g cooked = 65 kcal, P 3 g, C 13 g, F 0 g
- 1 katori (about 15 g): 15 g cooked = 50 kcal, P 2 g, C 10 g, F 0 g
- 30 g roasted in 1 tsp ghee: 30 g cooked + 5 g Ghee = 145 kcal, P 5 g, C 19 g, F 6 g
- 50 g: 50 g cooked = 165 kcal, P 8 g, C 32 g, F 1 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 330 kcal, P 15 g, C 65 g, F 2 g

Outside (Packaged or shop-roasted): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 2.5 to 10 g Groundnut oil = 165 to 230 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for packaged flavoured makhana was used. Packaged makhana is roasted in oil with seasoning; 1.5 to 3 times the fat of 30 g roasted in a teaspoon of ghee is a stated assumption. Check the packet's label.

Judgement calls:

- Makhana valued as USDA FDC 170149, lotus seeds, dried (332 kcal, 15.4 g protein, 1.97 g fat, 64.5 g carbohydrate per 100 g; no fibre value, so carbohydrate is by difference). Makhana is the seed of Euryale ferox, a different water lily from the lotus; USDA's dried lotus seed is the closest analysed match. IFCT 2017 has no entry.
- Katori 15 g is an assumption (half of USDA's 32 g cup, scaled for a 150 ml katori).
- No second analysed source for makhana was found in IFCT 2017, NIN Annexure 8 or USDA FNDDS.

Sources:

- USDA FoodData Central, Seeds, lotus seeds, dried (170149). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/170149/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Gulab jamun (/calories/gulab-jamun/)

Recipe: 100 g khoa and 25 g maida, fried and soaked in syrup, making 6 gulab jamuns.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Khoa (mawa) | 100 | 316.0 | 16.3 | 16.5 | 20.6 | IFCT 2017 L004 |
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 25 | 88.0 | 2.6 | 18.6 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Milk, whole, cow | 5 | 3.6 | 0.2 | 0.2 | 0.2 | IFCT 2017 L002 |
| Ghee | 11 | 97.3 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 11.0 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| Sugar, white | 108.3 | 419.1 | 0.0 | 108.3 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| **Total** | | **924.0** | **19.1** | **143.6** | **32.0** | |

Cooked yield 260 g (from protein: the dough's 19.1 g of protein is 7.35% of the finished gulab jamuns in a lab analysis, so 19.1 / 0.0735). Per 100 g cooked: 355.4 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 gulab jamun (about 43 g): 43 g cooked = 155 kcal, P 3 g, C 24 g, F 5 g
- 2 gulab jamuns: 87 g cooked = 310 kcal, P 6 g, C 48 g, F 11 g
- 1 with a tablespoon of syrup: 43 g cooked + 11.6 g Sugar, white = 200 kcal, P 3 g, C 36 g, F 5 g
- 100 g, drained: 100 g cooked = 355 kcal, P 7 g, C 55 g, F 12 g

Outside (Sweet shop or wedding): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.5 = 155 to 230 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for sweet-shop gulab jamun per piece was found. Pieces up to 1.5 times this size are a stated assumption; market brands analysed in the Indian Journal of Dairy Science had 6.5% to 12.3% fat, at or below this page's 12.3%.

Judgement calls:

- Finished weight from protein: khoa 16.34 + maida 2.59 + milk 0.16 = 19.09 g protein; at 7.35% (Rahuri 2020 control) that's 259.7 g for 6.
- Fat taken up: 12.32% x 259.7 = 32.0 g total; dough fat 21.0 g; so 11.0 g fried fat (ghee, as the textbook's 'fat').
- Sugar taken up: 48.16% x 259.7 = 125.1 g total sugar; minus milk sugar in khoa and milk (about 16.8 g, IFCT carbohydrate) = 108.3 g sucrose.
- Syrup on top: 1 tbsp (15 ml) of the textbook syrup (200 g sugar in 125 ml water, about 61.5% sugar, about 1.26 g per ml) = 11.6 g sugar, 45 kcal.
- No independent kcal figure for gulab jamun was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS). Market brands' fat (6.47% to 12.32%, IJDS) brackets this page's 12.3%.
- Ice cream in the changes list: NIN counts half a cup of ice cream at 200 kcal; a scoop is about half to two thirds of that.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Physico-chemical properties of gulabjamun (Rahuri trial, wheat flour control), Journal of Pharmacognosy and Phytochemistry, 2020. https://www.phytojournal.com/archives/2020/vol9issue3/PartAF/9-3-221-123.pdf (accessed 2026-10-11)
- Evaluation of selected physico-chemical, colour and textural characteristics of market gulabjamun, Indian Journal of Dairy Science. https://epubs.icar.org.in/index.php/IJDS/article/view/103349 (accessed 2026-10-11)
- USDA FoodData Central, Sugars, granulated (169655). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169655/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Rasgulla (/calories/rasgulla/)

Recipe: chhena from 265 ml cow's milk, cooked in syrup, making 4 rasgullas.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Paneer | 36.6 | 94.4 | 6.9 | 4.5 | 5.4 | IFCT 2017 L003 |
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 3.75 | 13.2 | 0.4 | 2.8 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Sugar, white | 45.8 | 177.2 | 0.0 | 45.8 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| **Total** | | **284.8** | **7.3** | **53.1** | **5.4** | |

Cooked yield 144 g (from protein: the chhena's 6.9 g of protein is 4.8% of the finished rasgullas in a lab analysis, so 6.9 / 0.048). Per 100 g cooked: 197.8 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 rasgulla (about 36 g): 36 g cooked = 70 kcal, P 2 g, C 13 g, F 1 g
- 2 rasgullas: 72 g cooked = 140 kcal, P 4 g, C 27 g, F 3 g
- 1 with a tablespoon of syrup: 36 g cooked + 11.6 g Sugar, white = 115 kcal, P 2 g, C 25 g, F 1 g
- 100 g, squeezed: 100 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 5 g, C 37 g, F 4 g

Outside (Tinned or sweet shop): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.4 = 70 to 100 kcal. Basis: Aavin's rasgulla label (273 kcal per 100 g) is about 1.4 times this page's value per gram, mainly from more sugar; tinned and shop rasgullas also vary in size. 1 to 1.4 times is used.

Cross-checks:

- Aavin rasgulla label, per 100 g (basis taken from the values): 273 kcal per 100 g, ours 198 (+38%).  https://aavin.tn.gov.in/sweets/-/asset_publisher/aUeiWRs5sODN/content/rasgulla

Range shown on page: Aavin's label (273 kcal, basis taken as 100 g) is 38% above this page's rasgulla, which is set to a lab analysis at 35% sugar. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Chhena: milk protein 265 x 3.26% = 8.64 g; casein about 80% of milk protein (assumption) = 6.91 g, as paneer (IFCT L003, 18.86% protein) = 36.6 g.
- Finished weight: 6.91 / 0.048 (VNMKV control rasgulla, 4.80% protein) = 144 g. Sugar: 34.96% x 144 = 50.3 g total; minus paneer's carbohydrate (4.5 g) = 45.8 g sucrose taken up.
- Fat check: 5.4 g / 144 g = 3.8%, against 4.5% in the analysis.
- 4 rasgullas of 36 g is an assumption; BFP392's serving count (2) is marked as guessed in INDB.
- Syrup on top: 1 tbsp of a 61.5% syrup = 11.6 g sugar, as on the gulab jamun page.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Studies on preparation of rasogolla by using isabgol, Vasantrao Naik Marathwada Krishi Vidyapeeth, Parbhani, 2017. https://krishikosh.egranth.ac.in/items/f751f1de-22f4-4b22-9c5f-afbfd1d438dd (accessed 2026-10-11)
- Aavin (Tamil Nadu Co-operative Milk Producers' Federation), Rasgulla. https://aavin.tn.gov.in/sweets/-/asset_publisher/aUeiWRs5sODN/content/rasgulla (accessed 2026-10-11)
- USDA FoodData Central, Sugars, granulated (169655). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169655/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Jalebi (/calories/jalebi/)

Recipe: 100 g maida batter with curd, fried and soaked in syrup.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheat flour, refined (maida) | 100 | 351.8 | 10.4 | 74.3 | 0.8 | IFCT 2017 A018 |
| Curd (dahi), whole milk | 25 | 15.3 | 0.9 | 1.2 | 0.8 | USDA FDC 171284 |
| Water | 60 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Groundnut oil | 46.2 | 408.5 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 46.2 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| Sugar, white | 117.8 | 455.9 | 0.0 | 117.8 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| Water | 73.7 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| **Total** | | **1231.5** | **11.2** | **193.2** | **47.8** | |

Cooked yield 383 g (fried weight (191.5 g, from the batter's solids at USDA's puri composition) plus an equal weight of syrup). Per 100 g cooked: 321.5 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 jalebi (about 30 g): 30 g cooked = 95 kcal, P 1 g, C 15 g, F 4 g
- 2 jalebis: 60 g cooked = 195 kcal, P 2 g, C 30 g, F 7 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 320 kcal, P 3 g, C 50 g, F 12 g
- 250 g (as sold by weight): 250 g cooked = 805 kcal, P 7 g, C 126 g, F 31 g

Outside (Halwai, with rabri): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 0 to 25 g Khoa (mawa), plus 0 to 5 g Sugar, white = 195 to 290 kcal. Basis: Rabri is thickened sweetened milk. 50 g of rabri is modelled as 25 g khoa and 5 g sugar (a stated assumption) on top of 2 jalebis.

Judgement calls:

- No analysed value for jalebi was found (IFCT 2017, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS, Europe PMC, web search). Consumer calorie databases list figures that don't reconcile and aren't used.
- Step 1, frying: batter non-fat solids = maida 100 x (1 - 0.1134 - 0.0076) = 87.9 g + curd 25 x (1 - 0.879 - 0.0325) = 2.2 g = 90.1 g. At FNDDS puri composition (24.94% fat, 27.99% water), fat = 90.1 x 24.94 / 47.07 = 47.7 g; minus own fat 1.6 g = 46.2 g oil; fried weight 90.1 / 0.4707 = 191.5 g.
- Step 2, syrup: 191.5 g of syrup at 200 / 325 = 61.5% sugar = 117.8 g sugar and 73.7 g water. Equal weight of syrup is an assumption.
- Rabri modelled as 25 g khoa + 5 g sugar per 50 g; an assumption.
- Piece weight 30 g is an assumption.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, FNDDS Bread, puri (2707714). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707714/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Yogurt, plain, whole milk (171284). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/171284/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Sugars, granulated (169655). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169655/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Kaju katli (/calories/kaju-katli/)

Recipe: 50 g cashews and 50 g sugar, making 6 pieces.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cashew nut | 50 | 291.4 | 9.4 | 12.7 | 22.6 | IFCT 2017 H005 |
| Sugar, white | 50 | 193.5 | 0.0 | 50.0 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| Water | 45 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Ghee | 5 | 44.2 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 5.0 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| **Total** | | **529.1** | **9.4** | **62.7** | **27.6** | |

Cooked yield 105 g (cashews, sugar and the greasing ghee, with the water cooked off). Per 100 g cooked: 503.9 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 sweet-shop piece (about 10 g): 10 g cooked = 50 kcal, P 1 g, C 6 g, F 3 g
- 1 home piece (a sixth, about 17 g): 18 g cooked = 90 kcal, P 2 g, C 10 g, F 5 g
- 4 sweet-shop pieces (40 g): 40 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 4 g, C 24 g, F 11 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 505 kcal, P 9 g, C 60 g, F 26 g

Outside (Packed or sweet shop): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 0.89 to 1 = 180 to 200 kcal. Basis: A packed kaju katli label (USDA FDC 740348) lists 450 kcal per 100 g, 11% below this recipe; shop recipes vary in the share of cashew to sugar. 0.89 to 1 times this recipe is used.

Cross-checks:

- Packed kaju katli label, USDA FDC 740348: 450 kcal per 100 g, ours 504 (-11%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/740348/nutrients

Judgement calls:

- Greasing ghee: INDB counts 2 tbsp (25.6 g) for 6 pieces; this page counts 5 g, an assumption for the film left on the pieces.
- Yield 105 g = 50 cashew + 50 sugar + 5 ghee, water cooked off.
- Sugar per 10 g piece: 50 / 105 x 10 = 4.8 g.
- Label cross-check per 100 g: 450 kcal (Mitha Magic, 40 g = 4 pieces).

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Besan ladoo (/calories/besan-ladoo/)

Recipe: 50 g besan and 10 g suji roasted in 30 g ghee with 50 g sugar, making 4 ladoos.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Besan (ground Bengal gram dal) | 50 | 164.6 | 10.8 | 23.4 | 2.7 | IFCT 2017 B001 |
| Semolina (suji, rava) | 10 | 33.4 | 1.1 | 6.8 | 0.1 | IFCT 2017 A022 |
| Ghee | 30 | 265.3 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 30.0 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| Sugar, white | 50 | 193.5 | 0.0 | 50.0 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| **Total** | | **656.7** | **11.9** | **80.2** | **32.7** | |

Cooked yield 140 g (the ingredients added together; roasting drives off little). Per 100 g cooked: 469.1 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 small ladoo (25 g): 25 g cooked = 115 kcal, P 2 g, C 14 g, F 6 g
- 1 ladoo (about 35 g): 35 g cooked = 165 kcal, P 3 g, C 20 g, F 8 g
- 2 ladoos: 70 g cooked = 330 kcal, P 6 g, C 40 g, F 16 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 470 kcal, P 9 g, C 57 g, F 23 g

Outside (Sweet shop (measured and large)): fat sources x 1.02 to 1, portion x 0.952 to 1.714 = 160 to 280 kcal. Basis: Low end calibrated to Gupta et al. 2016's besan ladoo from a Delhi vendor, 33.33 g with 7.93 g fat. High end is USDA FNDDS's 60 g ladoo piece at this recipe's composition.

Cross-checks:

- USDA FNDDS 2021-2023, Ladoo, round ball (91718300): 411 kcal per 100 g, ours 469 (-12%).  https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2710351/nutrients

Judgement calls:

- Fat check: this ladoo is (2.66 + 0.07 + 30) / 140 = 23.4% fat; Gupta et al.'s Delhi besan ladoo was 7.93 / 33.33 = 23.8%.
- Outside low: size 33.33 / 35 = 0.952, fat factor 1.02 to match 7.93 g fat. High: FNDDS 60 g ladoo piece, 60 / 35 = 1.714.
- Castor sugar valued as sugar. Cardamom and turmeric pinch left out.
- Ghee share: 265 / 657 kcal = 40%. Sugar per ladoo: 50 / 4 = 12.5 g.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Gupta et al., Unhealthy fat in street and snack foods, Journal of Nutrition Education and Behavior, 2016. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4826272 (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, FNDDS Ladoo, round ball (2710351). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2710351/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Sugars, granulated (169655). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169655/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Gajar halwa (/calories/gajar-halwa/)

Recipe: 200 g carrots with 40 g khoa, 40 g sugar and 20 g ghee, for 2 bowls.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Carrot, orange | 200 | 66.4 | 1.9 | 11.1 | 0.9 | IFCT 2017 F002 |
| Milk, whole, cow | 30 | 21.9 | 1.0 | 1.5 | 1.3 | IFCT 2017 L002 |
| Khoa (mawa) | 40 | 126.4 | 6.5 | 6.6 | 8.2 | IFCT 2017 L004 |
| Sugar, white | 40 | 154.8 | 0.0 | 40.0 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 169655 |
| Ghee | 20 | 176.9 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 20.0 | IFCT 2017 T013 |
| Almond | 2.2 | 13.4 | 0.4 | 0.1 | 1.3 | IFCT 2017 H001 |
| Cashew nut | 2.5 | 14.6 | 0.5 | 0.6 | 1.1 | IFCT 2017 H005 |
| **Total** | | **574.3** | **10.3** | **59.9** | **32.9** | |

No cooked weight used (no measured cooked weight; carrots lose a lot of water as they cook). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 small katori (a quarter of the recipe): 0.25 of the recipe = 145 kcal, P 3 g, C 15 g, F 8 g
- 1 bowl (half the recipe, 100 g carrots): 0.5 of the recipe = 285 kcal, P 5 g, C 30 g, F 16 g

Outside (Wedding or sweet shop): fat sources x 1 to 1.5, portion x 1 to 1, plus 0 to 20 g Khoa (mawa) = 285 to 395 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for sweet-shop gajar halwa was found. Up to 1.5 times the ghee and up to 20 g more khoa per bowl are stated assumptions.

Judgement calls:

- Pistachios (0.4 g) and cardamom left out.
- Ghee, sugar and khoa: (176.9 + 154.8 + 126.4) / 574.4 = 80% of calories; with milk and nuts, about 90%.
- No analysed value for gajar halwa per bowl was found (IFCT, NIN Annexure 8, USDA FNDDS). NIN's halwa (kesari, 320 kcal per half cup) is a semolina halwa and isn't used.
- Ice cream: NIN counts half a cup at 200 kcal; a scoop is about half to two thirds of that.
- Changes list: nuts 10 g x about 580 to 610 kcal per 100 g (IFCT almond H001, cashew H005) = about 60 kcal.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Sugars, granulated (169655). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169655/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Coffee, brewed | 50 | 0.5 | 0.1 | 0.0 | 0.0 | USDA FDC 171890 |
| Milk, whole, cow | 100 | 72.9 | 3.3 | 4.9 | 4.5 | IFCT 2017 L002 |
| **Total** | | **73.4** | **3.3** | **4.9** | **4.5** | |

Cooked yield 150 g (the decoction and milk together). Per 100 g cooked: 48.9 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 tumbler, no sugar: 150 g cooked = 75 kcal, P 3 g, C 5 g, F 4 g
- 1 tumbler, 1 tsp sugar: 150 g cooked + 4.2 g Sugar, white = 90 kcal, P 3 g, C 9 g, F 4 g
- 1 tumbler, 2 tsp sugar: 150 g cooked + 8.4 g Sugar, white = 105 kcal, P 3 g, C 13 g, F 4 g
- 1 tumbler, toned milk, 2 tsp sugar: 0 of the recipe + 50 g Coffee, brewed + 100 g Milk, toned (3% fat) + 8.4 g Sugar, white = 90 kcal, P 3 g, C 13 g, F 3 g

Outside (Darshini or Udupi hotel): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1.2, plus 0 to 4.2 g Sugar, white = 105 to 145 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for hotel filter coffee was found. Up to a teaspoon more sugar and a serving up to 1.2 times as big are stated assumptions.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: coffee (2 tsp sugar + 100 ml milk), 1 cup: 110 kcal per a tumbler, ours 106 (+4%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Judgement calls:

- Tumbler = 50 ml decoction + 100 ml milk, NIN's coffee milk amount. Whole cow milk (IFCT L002) used; NIN doesn't state the milk.
- Decoction valued as USDA brewed coffee (FDC 171890), about 1 kcal per 100 g.
- Buffalo milk in the changes list: 100 ml x (107.3 - 72.9) = 34 kcal more; toned: 72.9 - 58 = 15 kcal less.

## Chaas (/calories/chaas/)

Recipe: 100 g curd whisked with about 320 ml water and ice, with cumin.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Curd (dahi), whole milk | 100 | 61.0 | 3.5 | 4.7 | 3.3 | USDA FDC 171284 |
| Water | 320 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | no calories |
| Cumin seeds | 0.3 | 0.9 | 0.0 | 0.1 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G025 |
| **Total** | | **61.9** | **3.5** | **4.7** | **3.3** | |

Cooked yield 420 g (the ingredients added together). Per 100 g cooked: 14.7 kcal. Katori density 1 g per ml (taken as 1 g per ml, like water).

Portions:

- 1 glass (200 ml): 200 g cooked = 30 kcal, P 2 g, C 2 g, F 2 g
- 1 large glass (250 ml): 250 g cooked = 35 kcal, P 2 g, C 3 g, F 2 g

Judgement calls:

- Ice 13.5 tbsp (INDB) taken as about 200 g, plus 0.5 cup (120 ml) water = 320 g.
- Thick chaas: a 200 ml glass of half curd = 100 g curd; the base glass has 47.6 g, so 52.4 g added.
- No independent analysed value for Indian chaas was found. USDA's cultured buttermilk (43 kcal per 100 g) is a thicker, different drink and isn't used.

- 1 glass, 2 tsp sugar: 0.5 of the recipe + 8.4 g Sugar, white = 40 kcal, P 0 g, C 8 g, F 0 g
- 1 glass, 1½ tbsp sugar (textbook): 0.5 of the recipe + 18.75 g Sugar, white = 80 kcal, P 0 g, C 19 g, F 0 g
- 1 glass, 1 tsp honey: 0.5 of the recipe + 7 g Honey = 30 kcal, P 0 g, C 6 g, F 0 g

Outside (Street shikanji or nimbu soda): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 8.4 to 16.8 g Sugar, white = 40 to 75 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for street shikanji was found. 2 to 4 level teaspoons of sugar per glass is a stated assumption; ICMR-NIN's fresh lime juice, 60 kcal a glass, sits inside it.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: fresh lime juice, 1 glass: 60 kcal per sweet, ours 82 (-26%).  https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf

Range shown on page: NIN's fresh lime juice (60 kcal) is 22% below the textbook's sweet glass and well above an unsweetened one. Sugar differs. Shown as a range.

Judgement calls:

- Lemon '1 no': INDB records 30 g of flesh; taken here as 48 g of juice (USDA FDC 167747).
- Sugar 3 tbsp for 2 glasses = 37.5 g (USDA tbsp 12.5 g), 18.75 g a glass.
- Honey 1 tsp = 7 g (USDA 1 tbsp = 21 g).
- NIN's 60 kcal glass: (60 - 4.4) / 3.87 = about 14 g of sugar, 3.4 level teaspoons.
- Crushed ice left out; it adds no calories.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- Vijayakumar et al., Indian Nutrient Databank (INDB), Current Developments in Nutrition, 2024. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11277795/ (accessed 2026-10-10)
- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians: A Manual (2011). https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Sugars, granulated (169655). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169655/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Honey (169640). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/169640/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)
- USDA FoodData Central, Lemon juice, raw (167747). https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/167747/nutrients (accessed 2026-10-10)

## Boiled egg (/calories/boiled-egg/)

Recipe: one hen's egg, about 50 g without the shell, boiled.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Egg, hen, whole, raw | 50 | 67.4 | 6.6 | 0.0 | 4.6 | IFCT 2017 M001 |
| **Total** | | **67.4** | **6.6** | **0.0** | **4.6** | |

Cooked yield 50 g (boiling in the shell doesn't change the weight of the egg). Per 100 g cooked: 134.8 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 boiled egg (about 50 g): 50 g cooked = 65 kcal, P 7 g, C 0 g, F 5 g
- 2 boiled eggs: 100 g cooked = 135 kcal, P 13 g, C 0 g, F 9 g
- 3 boiled eggs: 150 g cooked = 200 kcal, P 20 g, C 0 g, F 14 g

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: boiled egg, 1: 90 kcal per an egg, ours 67 (+34%). NIN gives no weight; 90 kcal fits an egg of about 65 g. https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FoodData Central 173424, egg, hard-boiled, 1 large (50 g): 77.5 kcal per an egg, ours 67 (+15%). USDA's egg has a little more fat than IFCT's. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/173424/nutrients

Range shown on page: Egg size: ICMR-NIN's 90 kcal fits a bigger egg than the 50 g one counted here, and USDA's egg carries a little more fat than IFCT's.

Judgement calls:

- Egg weight is the Indian Nutrient Databank's household measure (1 egg = 50 g), the same as USDA's large egg.
- No outside range is shown: a boiled egg bought from a stall is the same egg. An egg bhurji or omelette stall is a different dish, with its own page.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-11)

## Omelette (/calories/omelette/)

Recipe: a plain 2-egg omelette with a tablespoon of milk, cooked in oil.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Egg, hen, whole, raw | 100 | 134.8 | 13.3 | 0.0 | 9.2 | IFCT 2017 M001 |
| Milk, whole, cow | 15 | 10.9 | 0.5 | 0.7 | 0.7 | IFCT 2017 L002 |
| Black pepper | 0.5 | 1.1 | 0.1 | 0.2 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G031 |
| Groundnut oil | 7.6 | 67.2 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 7.6 | IFCT 2017 T005 |
| **Total** | | **214.0** | **13.8** | **0.9** | **17.4** | |

Cooked yield 110 g (USDA's omelette made with oil weighs 55 g per egg). Per 100 g cooked: 194.6 kcal.

Portions:

- 1 omelette (2 eggs): 110 g cooked = 215 kcal, P 14 g, C 1 g, F 17 g
- 1 single-egg omelette: 55 g cooked = 105 kcal, P 7 g, C 0 g, F 9 g
- 1 omelette (3 eggs): 165 g cooked = 320 kcal, P 21 g, C 1 g, F 26 g

Outside (Stall or dhaba masala omelette): fat sources x 1.5 to 2.5, portion x 1 to 1, plus 0 to 25 g Onion, big, plus 0 to 25 g Tomato, ripe, hybrid = 250 to 330 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for a stall omelette was found. ICMR-NIN's fried egg at 160 kcal implies roughly 10 g of oil per egg, about 2.5 times the oil taken up here, so 1.5 to 2.5 times the oil is a stated assumption. Onion and tomato up to 25 g each cover a masala omelette.

Cross-checks:

- ICMR-NIN, Dietary Guidelines for Indians (2011), Annexure 8: fried egg, 1: 160 kcal per a 1-egg omelette, ours 107 (+50%). A fried egg, the closest NIN item; NIN gives no weight or oil. https://nin.res.in/downloads/DietaryGuidelinesforNINwebsite.pdf
- USDA FoodData Central 2707200, egg omelet made with oil: 192 kcal per 100 g, ours 195 (-1%). The analysis the oil is set from; USDA's eggs carry slightly more fat than IFCT's. https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/food-details/2707200/nutrients

Range shown on page: ICMR-NIN's fried egg (160 kcal) has more oil than a single-egg omelette made this way; USDA's analysed omelette (192 kcal per 100 g) agrees within 2%.

Judgement calls:

- The textbook fries the omelette in 2 tablespoons of fat, most of which stays in the pan; the oil eaten is set from USDA's analysed omelette instead.
- An omelette is counted as 2 eggs, as in the textbook. A single-egg omelette is half.

Sources:

- ICMR-NIN, Indian Food Composition Tables 2017. https://nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf (accessed 2026-10-11)

Cooked yield 55 g (USDA's scrambled egg weighs 55 g per egg). Per 100 g cooked: 205.7 kcal. Katori density 0.57 g per ml (USDA's scrambled egg: 135 g per cup (236.6 ml)).

| Garlic | 0.59 | 0.7 | 0.0 | 0.1 | 0.0 | IFCT 2017 G011 |

No cooked weight used (no cooked weight is known for the bhaturas, so the plate is counted as one portion). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 plate (2 bhaturas, 1 katori chole): 1 of the recipe = 635 kcal, P 15 g, C 67 g, F 32 g
- 1 bhatura with half a katori of chole: 0.5 of the recipe = 315 kcal, P 8 g, C 34 g, F 16 g

Outside (Chole bhature stall or restaurant): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 4.5 to 9 g Groundnut oil = 675 to 715 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for a plate of chole bhature was found. The chole page's outside range uses 1.5 to 2 times its 2 teaspoons of oil, added here as 4.5 to 9 g of oil. The bhaturas are left as they are, because the two street bhaturas measured in the lab (see the bhatura page) held the same or less fat than the home bhatura counted here.

Judgement calls:

- A plate of 2 bhaturas and one 200 ml katori of chole is a stated assumption: no source was found for a standard plate. Onion, pickle and a green chilli on the side add little.

## Curd (/calories/curd/)

Plain curd (dahi) made from whole milk.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Curd (dahi), whole milk | 100 | 61.0 | 3.5 | 4.7 | 3.3 | USDA FDC 171284 |
| **Total** | | **61.0** | **3.5** | **4.7** | **3.3** | |

Cooked yield 100 g (curd as it is). Per 100 g cooked: 61.0 kcal. Katori density 1.04 g per ml (USDA's plain whole-milk yogurt: 245 g per cup (236.6 ml)).

Portions:

- 1 katori (150 ml): 156 g cooked = 95 kcal, P 5 g, C 7 g, F 5 g
- 1 katori (200 ml): 208 g cooked = 125 kcal, P 7 g, C 10 g, F 7 g
- 100 g: 100 g cooked = 60 kcal, P 3 g, C 5 g, F 3 g

Judgement calls:

- IFCT 2017 has no curd, so the values are USDA's plain whole-milk yogurt, as on the site's curd rice and raita pages. Curd from toned milk has about two thirds of the fat.
- No cross-check is listed: the only analysed value for plain curd is USDA's yogurt, which is the value used, and IFCT 2017 and NIN's Annexure 8 have no plain curd entry that states a weight. Shop curd labels in India commonly list 60 to 100 kcal per 100 g depending on the milk, which brackets this value.
- No outside range is shown: curd served with a meal outside is the same curd, set from whatever milk the kitchen uses.

## Maggi (/calories/maggi/)

One 70 g pack of Maggi 2-Minute Masala Noodles with its tastemaker, cooked in water as the pack says.

| Ingredient | Raw g | kcal | Protein g | Carbs g | Fat g | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Instant masala noodles (Maggi), dry, with tastemaker | 70 | 268.8 | 5.7 | 41.7 | 8.8 | Maggi label |
| **Total** | | **268.8** | **5.7** | **41.7** | **8.8** | |

No cooked weight used (cooked in water, which adds weight and no calories). Portions are shares of the recipe.

Portions:

- 1 pack (70 g): 1 of the recipe = 270 kcal, P 6 g, C 42 g, F 9 g
- 2 packs: 2 of the recipe = 540 kcal, P 11 g, C 83 g, F 18 g
- 1 pack with 1 egg: 1 of the recipe + 50 g Egg, hen, whole, raw = 335 kcal, P 12 g, C 42 g, F 13 g
- 1 pack, 1 tsp butter (5 g): 1 of the recipe + 5 g Butter = 305 kcal, P 6 g, C 42 g, F 13 g
- 1 pack with vegetables (25 g each of onion, carrot and capsicum): 1 of the recipe + 25 g Onion, big + 25 g Carrot, orange + 25 g Capsicum, green = 295 kcal, P 7 g, C 46 g, F 9 g

Outside (Maggi point or canteen): fat sources x 1 to 1, portion x 1 to 1, plus 5 to 10 g Butter, plus 25 to 25 g Onion, big, plus 0 to 25 g Tomato, ripe, hybrid = 315 to 355 kcal. Basis: No analysed value for stall Maggi was found. A stall or canteen cooks the same pack in butter with onion and often tomato; 1 to 2 teaspoons of butter and 25 to 50 g of vegetables are stated assumptions. Cheese, where added, is extra.

Judgement calls:

- Values are Nestlé India's own, per 100 g as sold (noodle cake and tastemaker). maggi.in lists a 75 g serve; the pack label says 70 g, which is used here.
- Butter, cheese or vegetables added while cooking are extra: a teaspoon of butter adds about 35 kcal.
- No independent analysed value for Maggi exists; the only values are Nestlé India's own, so there is no cross-check. maggi.in lists a 75 g serve at 288 calories, while the single pack sold in India weighs 70 g, which is what is counted here. The Open Food Facts record for this pack carries only a photo of the label, so it isn't cited.

Sources:
